# Decisions — 001-faster-slipwai

## D1 — How does this repository integrate today? (Integration row `unrecorded`, rung `unknown`)
- **Stage:** ground · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:47:02Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The Ground stage asks a person to place the Integration row before a slice that changes how slices merge and how `main` moves (FR-010). Nobody is at the wheel. What does the run assume about how work reaches `main` here today?
- **Options:** (a) stop and wait for a person; (b) mark the row `confirmed` from the tree — not available, a fact about the world is not a decision (cruise.md stop 11); (c) leave the row exactly as it is and work on a stated assumption drawn from the tree — recommended by cruise.md stop 11.
- **Decision:** (c). The Integration row stays `unrecorded` at `unknown` in `project.json` and `delivery/docs/convergence.md`. The run works on this assumption, named as one: changes reach `main` through short topic-branch pull requests (`fix/…`, `feat/…`, `docs/…`) merged with a merge commit after CI has run on the pull request; `main` is pushed to directly only by `make release`, whose Release and Open commits and `v*` tag land in one atomic push; `verify.yml` and `verify-delivery.yml` run on every pull request and on every push to `main` on this forge. That is the ladder's `branches` rung with CI on every commit — not `trunk`, because branch lifetime is unmeasured, and not `continuous`, because whether a red build stops the line depends on forge settings (branch protection, required checks) the tree cannot show. FR-010's fast-forward-only `main` is something this feature builds for the merge tree generated projects run, reaching this repository through `slipwai migrate`; the run does not assume it already holds here.
- **Why:** `AGENTS.md` *Versioning*: `main` carries the next release as a `.dev` snapshot and every green push publishes it; *Delivery method*: one pull request per slice. `project.json` `ci`: forge `github`, branch `main`, gate `.github/workflows/verify-delivery.yml`, provenance `overridden`. `.github/workflows/verify.yml` lines 8–18: triggers are `push` to `main`, `v*` tags and `pull_request`, and its `concurrency` block never cancels a `main` run. `.github/workflows/verify-delivery.yml`: `push` to `main` and `pull_request`, running `make -f delivery/Makefile verify`. `git log --first-parent main`: 28 of the last 30 first-parent commits are `Merge pull request … into main`; the other two are `Release 1.5.1` and `Open 1.5.2.dev0`. Also seen and left alone: `verify.yml` and `publish-package.yml` name `https://git.treyco.dev` as the canonical forge and GitHub as a mirror; `ci.forge` is a person's `overridden` value and is not touched by this entry.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person places the Integration row through `/ground` — the rung, whether required checks protect `main`, and the measured branch lifetime — or says they cannot place it today.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D2 — Is the test suite green and trusted? (Safety net `detected` at `tests-exist`)
- **Stage:** ground · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:47:02Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The row is the tree's reading and nobody's answer. What does the run assume about the suite it changes code beside?
- **Options:** (a) stop and ask; (b) mark the row `confirmed` or raise it to `tests-pass` from CI history — not available, the rung is a person's to place; (c) work on the detected value as a stated assumption — recommended by cruise.md stop 11.
- **Decision:** (c). The row stays `detected` at `tests-exist`. The run works on this assumption: `make test` is the recorded test command and runs the unittest suite (829 tests, per the constitution's note under principle V); whether it is green in the gate with no quarantine is not claimed. Every slice therefore establishes green on its own tree through `make -f delivery/Makefile verify` before it pushes, as principle V requires, and no test is quarantined, skipped or moved to get there.
- **Why:** `project.json` `deployables.slipwai-graph.commands.test` is `make test`, provenance `confirmed`; the row's evidence is `test recorded for slipwai-graph`. `verify.yml` runs `make verify`, tests included, on every pull request and `main` push — which suggests `tests-pass`, but the ladder says that rung is established by a person reading the gate, not inferred by the run.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person confirms the row at `tests-exist`, raises it to `tests-pass` having read the gate, or overrides it with a quarantine the run must respect.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`
- **Status:** overridden by D11

## D3 — What is this repository, structurally? (Structure `detected` at `named`)
- **Stage:** ground · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:47:02Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The row is the tree's reading and nobody's answer. What layout does the run work within?
- **Options:** (a) stop and ask; (b) mark the row `confirmed` — not available; (c) work on the detected value as a stated assumption — recommended by cruise.md stop 11.
- **Decision:** (c). The row stays `detected` at `named`. The run works on this assumption: `slipwai-graph` is one deployable of kind `tool` at `.`, and slices keep its layout — `src/slipwai/`, `assets/`, `delivery/`, `tests/` — within `make check-structure`'s import direction and per-module budgets. No slice in this feature moves the tool under `apps/` or opens a ports-and-adapters split: the constitution says none is planned under principles IV and XV. Changes to what the factory generates land under `assets/` and `src/slipwai/project/` first (owner brief, priority 3) and reach this repository through `slipwai migrate`.
- **Why:** `project.json` `deployables.slipwai-graph`: `kind: tool` and `purpose` both `confirmed`, `path: "."`; the row's evidence is `slipwai-graph: tool; not under apps/: .`; `.specify/memory/constitution.md` principle IV: *no slice is planned for it in Faster Slipwai*; principle XV and the typing constraint say the same of `typed`.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person confirms or overrides the row, or asks for a `laid-out` or `hexagonal` slice on the map's Structure row.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D5 — Which change strategy does the run proceed on? (Strategy `recommended: leave-it`, nothing decided)
- **Stage:** ground · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:47:02Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The map recommends `leave-it` and says the decision is a person's, written as an accepted ADR. Nobody is at the wheel, and the owner brief lists accepting that ADR under *Always ask a person*.
- **Options:** `leave-it` (recommended by the survey); `in-place`; `modular-monolith`; `strangler-fig`; `rewrite` (never recommended here). Then: (a) stop and ask; (b) write the ADR as `Accepted` — not available, the word is a person's; (c) draft the ADR at `Proposed` and proceed on the recommendation — recommended by cruise.md stop 12.
- **Decision:** (c). `delivery/docs/adr/0002-change-strategy.md` is written in Nygard's five sections at Status `Proposed`, carrying the one line `Strategy: leave-it`. The run proceeds on that recommendation: no capability moves, no routing seam, no retirement ledger, no rewrite; the feature's work is delivery slices landing first in what the factory generates. The Strategy row stays `recommended`, provenance `detected`; `/survey` will not read the ADR as a decision until a person changes its Status.
- **Why:** The trigger — a faster delivery loop with its gates intact — names no platform, delivery, change, capability or host problem for an architectural strategy to answer (`project.json` `strategy.because`); the constitution plans no structural slice in this feature (IV, XV); the owner brief rules out any MAJOR change and replacing the harness or the ladder. `src/slipwai/strategy.py` reads a strategy only from an ADR whose `## Status` is `Accepted`, so a `Proposed` draft changes no record.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person accepts the ADR (the row then moves to `decided` and, with no ledger to empty, `done`), or chooses another strategy in a superseding ADR.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`, `delivery/docs/adr/0002-change-strategy.md`
- **Status:** overridden by human 2026-10-03

## D6 — Do the programme's quick wins come first? (four "connection string with a password" hits, seven "no lockfile" hits)
- **Stage:** ground · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:47:02Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The ladder says a secret in the tree is a slice of its own, first, whatever else is planned, and the programme lists eleven `quick-win` rows. Is any of the four flagged lines a credential, and are the seven missing lockfiles a step this run takes ahead of the feature?
- **Options:** (a) open a secret-rotation slice first, as the pacing says; (b) read each flagged line and decide from what is written there — recommended by this stop's brief; (c) commit a lockfile beside each of the seven `package.json`; (d) take none; (e) take the one the feature already owns, inside its own slice.
- **Decision:** (b) for the secrets, then (d) for them: none of the four hits is a credential, so no rotation, no purge of history and no secret slice. (e) for the lockfiles: six of the seven sit where a lockfile beside the file would be wrong or where the factory keeps it elsewhere; the seventh — the event-model tooling — is FR-003's own deliverable (`check-drawio` skips `npm install` when the installed tree matches a committed lockfile) and is taken inside the User Story 1 slice that owns it, not as a quick win ahead of it. Nothing in the tree is rotated, purged, rewritten or committed by this entry; the programme rows stay as `/survey` derived them, and the detector that raised them (`src/slipwai/quick_wins.py`, `SHAPES`) is left as it is — teaching it the placeholder rule it already applies to keyed literals is product work for the host to plan, not a workaround for this stop.
- **Why:** Each line, read in place:
  - `assets/backing-services/java/database_url.java:10` — a Javadoc comment: `{@code postgres://user:pass@host:port/db}`, the documented shape of `DATABASE_URL` with every part a placeholder word. Not a secret.
  - `assets/backing-services/java/tests/database_url_test.java:19` — `DatabaseUrl.parse("postgres://app:secret@localhost:5433/app")`, the fixture a parser test splits into JDBC URL, username and password; the host is `localhost` and the password is the word `secret`. Not a secret.
  - `tests/test_release.py:299` — `module.web_url("https://someone:a-token@git.example/owner/slipwai.git")`, a fixture proving `web_url` strips credentials from a remote URL; `.example` is a reserved name. Not a secret.
  - `tests/test_upgrade.py:187` — the first line of a test docstring: `pip --index-url https://user:token@forge/...`, illustrating the case the test covers; the test's own value is `someone:a-token` against its local test server. Not a secret.
  - The detector matches the shape `scheme://user:pass@host` without the placeholder test it applies to keyed literals, which is why four placeholders read as secrets.
  - `assets/frontends/react-vite/api-client/package.json` and `assets/frontends/react-vite/app/package.json` — templates the generator merges per variant; the lockfile a project gets is written from `assets/frontends/react-vite/locks/<variant>.json` (`src/slipwai/project/frontend.py:172`, `:223`), ten committed variants. A lock beside the template would be a copy nothing reads. Not a step.
  - `assets/languages/typescript/app/package.json` — the same pattern: four committed locks under `assets/languages/typescript/locks/`. Not a step.
  - `assets/toolkit/scripts/event-model/package.json` — exact pins (`yaml` 2.9.0, `zod` 4.4.3, `tsx` 4.23.12), installed by `make model` in a generated project; no lockfile is committed for it anywhere, and `src/slipwai/ecosystems.py:89` chooses `npm ci` only when one is present. Real, and owned by FR-003 of this feature: taken in User Story 1's slice.
  - `tests/fixtures/adopt/converging/apps/shop/package.json`, `tests/fixtures/adopt/javascript-gitlab/package.json`, `tests/fixtures/adopt/javascript-service/package.json` — checked-in fixture repositories for the adopt tests; `tests/test_adopt.py:59` says a lockfile-less JavaScript service is deliberate so an install needs no registry, and `tests/test_quick_wins.py:69` asserts that the `no-lockfile` finding is raised. A lock here would change what the tests prove. Not a step.
  - The pacing text on the seven lockfile rows reads *a secret in the tree today*, which is the quick-win pacing shared across kinds, not a claim about those files.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person reads one of the four lines and says it is a live credential, or wants the event-model lockfile taken as a slice of its own ahead of User Story 1.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D7 — What is every slice's release constraint under `release: flagged`?
- **Stage:** split · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:52:46Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The release-constraint stage asks what hides a merged slice from a real actor until a person says it ships. Here the product is a factory: a merge to `main` publishes a `1.5.2.dev<N>` snapshot and a generated project only changes when its maintainer runs `slipwai migrate`. What constraint does every slice in the split carry?
- **Options:** (a) a feature flag per slice inside the generated toolkit — recommended against by the stage, since nothing here runs in production and a flag would be a new setting with no reader; (b) the standing one: the change lands on `main` as a pre-release snapshot no installer takes unasked, reaches a project only through `slipwai migrate` run by its maintainer, and becomes a release only through `make release`, which this run never runs — recommended by the stage, as the smallest constraint that already holds; (c) park at the first push.
- **Decision:** (b). Every slice's *Release Constraint* column in `story-split.md` says so; a slice that adds a setting ships it with a documented default (owner brief, *Taste*) and the run never flips one. `make release`, a `v*` tag and raising `VERSION` to a MAJOR stay a person's.
- **Why:** `AGENTS.md` *Versioning*: a `.dev` version is a pre-release every installer passes over unless asked, and `make release` is the only thing that writes a release. The owner brief's *Out of scope* rules out switching routing on and any MAJOR; the cruise rule *Flags stay off* is satisfied by the snapshot's own semantics.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person asks for a snapshot to be held back from `main` as well, in which case slices accumulate on a long-lived branch and the constraint becomes that branch.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D8 — Where do the method slices and the unplanned map rows go in the split?
- **Stage:** split · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:52:46Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The ladder offers the programme top-first and then the lowest map row still below target; the split places method slices among the product slices, never ahead of all of them by default, a secret and an open CRITICAL excepted. D6 found no secret. What is placed, and where?
- **Options:** (a) one method slice per row below target, ahead of the product (safety net, structure, platform, constitution, integration, strategy) — against the split's rule and the owner's priority 3; (b) only the step a product slice waits on, first, and the programme's one cheap tooling step, last — recommended by the stage; (c) no method slices at all — refused by the Pin stage, which will not take a slice while `running.md` reads *Not yet proven*.
- **Decision:** (b). `S00-run-path` goes first: it proves the recorded `smoke`, writes `delivery/survey/running.md`, and claims `safety-net: tests-exist → tests-pass` from a green gate, because every product slice changes code that was here and the Pin stage refuses them until then. `S19-pip-audit` (`platform: supported → audited`) goes last, taken earlier only when a fan-out has a free seat. Structure (`named → laid-out`), the rest of the safety-net ladder, and the Constitution row are not planned in this run; the Parking Lot in `story-split.md` says why for each.
- **Why:** owner brief, priority 3 (the generated project's loop over the factory's own convenience) and *Out of scope* (this repository's own suite beyond what the general changes give it); `delivery/commands/drive.md` stage 7 (the application has to start before any pin) and stage 9 (a method slice is placed by the split, never ahead of all product slices by default); `project.json` `strategy.programme` has one step left after D6, the `audit` command.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person asks for a map row to move in this run, or an open CRITICAL appears in `adversary-log.md`, which pre-empts every slice.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D9 — Does this repository take each change through `slipwai migrate` inside the run (E8)?
- **Stage:** split · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T01:52:46Z · **Iteration:** 1
- **Question:** The spec's *Assumptions* say the factory's own repository takes these changes through `slipwai migrate` after each lands, so the run speeds up as it goes. Can an iteration do that?
- **Options:** (a) run `./slipwai migrate` here after each slice merges — it rewrites files under `delivery/scripts/`, which the runner treats as a changed control and parks the run on, whatever the last line said; (b) leave E8 to a person between runs, and let this repository's loop run the gate it has today — recommended, as the only option that does not park; (c) exempt migrate from the control check — changing the gate, on the catastrophic list.
- **Decision:** (b). No iteration runs `slipwai migrate` on this repository. The split's Parking Lot names E8 as a person's step; the cruise report will list it as the first thing to do when the run ends.
- **Why:** `delivery/commands/cruise.md`, *A failing gate is never repaired in the gate*: the runner compares `delivery/scripts/`, `tools/`, the `Makefile`, CI and hook settings before and after every iteration and parks on any change; `delivery/.written` lists the files migrate replaces, and `delivery/scripts/` is among them.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person runs the migrate themselves between iterations, or the factory learns to migrate without touching a control the runner watches.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D11 — Does S00 flip the Safety net row to `tests-pass`, and with what provenance?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S00-run-path · **When:** 2026-10-03T02:42:00Z · **Iteration:** 2
- **Question:** D2 left the row `detected` at `tests-exist` and said the rung is a person's to place having read the gate; D8 planned S00 to claim `tests-exist → tests-pass` from a green gate; the slice's second example says *the convergence row's evidence names the run*. Who flips the row, to what, and with which provenance?
- **Options:** (a) leave the row where it is and only record the green runs in this file for a person to flip — keeps D2 to the letter, but S00 then reaches no rung and the map says nothing the slice proved; (b) flip it at the Convergence stage the way `delivery/commands/drive.md` stage 9 says — rung `tests-pass`, `evidence` naming what established it, provenance `confirmed`, `planned` cleared — with the evidence saying in words that a cruise iteration and not a person read the gate (recommended by the stage); (c) flip it with provenance `detected` — not available: that is the survey's word and `/survey` rewrites a `detected` row from the tree.
- **Decision:** (b). At S00's Convergence stage, once `make verify` and `make -f delivery/Makefile verify` are green on the slice's final commit with `CRUISE_RUNNER` and `CRUISE_ITERATION` set, `project.json`'s `safety-net` row is edited to rung `tests-pass`, provenance `confirmed`, `planned` null, evidence naming the two commands, the commit, and *established by cruise iteration 2; no person has read the gate*; then `/survey` regenerates the page and `make -f delivery/Makefile check-convergence` holds it. Nothing is quarantined, skipped or moved to get there (D2's working rule stands as AC-S00-5).
- **Why:** `delivery/commands/drive.md` stage 9: *a rung this slice reached — a quarantined suite now green … — flips its row: edit that row in `project.json` (rung, evidence naming what established it, provenance `confirmed`, planned cleared), run `/survey`*; the rung's definition in `src/slipwai/convergence.py:59` is a fact the gate establishes, *the suite is green in the gate, with no quarantine*, and `check-convergence` contradicts `tests-pass` from `delivery/baseline.json` when the suite is quarantined (`src/slipwai/project/ground_command.py:36`), so a person can check the claim from the tree rather than take it on trust; D2's own reversal clause names raising the row to `tests-pass` having read the gate, and the gate's output is in the slice's evidence for them to read.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person sets the row back or says only a person's reading moves the Safety net row — the row then returns to `tests-exist`, provenance `detected`, and the green runs stay recorded in this entry and in the slice's `tasks.md`.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S00-5, AC-S00-6); `project.json` and `delivery/docs/convergence.md` at the Convergence stage
- **Status:** standing

## D12 — On which branch do S00-run-path's increment commits land, and how are they integrated, while the checkout is the unmerged, unpushed `adopt-method`?
- **Stage:** plan · **Slice:** S00-run-path · **When:** 2026-10-03T02:48:46Z · **Iteration:** 2
- **Question:** The ladder's shape is a `slice/<id>` branch claimed on the forge, demoed unpushed, then pushed and merged into `main` with Phase 4 on `main`. The run was started on `adopt-method` — the person's adoption branch, never pushed, 7 commits ahead of `origin/main` and 0 behind — and the adoption has not been merged. Where do S00's commits go, how are they integrated, and what holds for the slices after it?
- **Options:** (a) work S00 directly on `adopt-method`: no `slice/` branch, no claim, no push; the register's *Merged as* names the commits — recommended by the stage; (b) `slice/S00-run-path` cut from `adopt-method`, merged back `--no-ff` — `merge_base()` resolves to `main` at `e1a9e43`, so the slice's diff is the adoption's 326 files and the gate is red for a reason the slice's tree cannot fix; (c) fast-forward `main` to `adopt-method`, push, follow the ladder against `main` — takes the person's step 6 of `adoption.md` for them and publishes a `1.5.2.dev<N>` snapshot through CI; (d) fast-forward the *local* `main` only — moves a ref in the person's checkout they did not ask to move and leaves it diverged from `origin/main`.
- **Decision:** (a), the stage's recommendation, taken. S00's increment commits land on `adopt-method`, on top of `5460bf9`, in the shape the ladder gives every commit (RED-GREEN-REFACTOR increments, then the convergence commit). No `slice/S00-run-path` branch is cut, nothing is pushed to `origin` under any name this slice, and the board's 🔀 *Ready* line says *the local branch is the claim; the forge's `main` does not carry the adoption*, as `drive.md` says to when the claim is local. The demo runs from `adopt-method` at the slice's tip — unpushed, and not `main`. After acceptance, Phase 4 (adversary, mutation, `make -f delivery/Makefile verify`, marking done) runs on `adopt-method` too; the register row's *Merged as* names the first and last S00 commit, so a person can later cut them into their own PR (`git branch slice/S00-run-path <last>` and open the adoption's PR from `5460bf9`) and the one-PR-per-slice rule stays satisfiable even though this run cannot satisfy it. **Merging the adoption to `main`, and pushing `adopt-method`, are `unavailable: a person's approval`** — `adoption.md` step 6 addresses that merge to the person, and a push to `main` publishes (D7); the run does neither. **For slices after S00, until a person acts:** one slice at a time, in split order, on `adopt-method`, under exactly these terms — no `slice/<id>` branches, no claims, no pushes, worktree fan-out not used; the spec's own edge case (*a harness that cannot delegate: the fan-out degrades to one slice at a time*) is the shape, and the model line says `harness cannot delegate: adoption unmerged`. This entry does not override D7: the snapshot stays the release constraint, and slices sitting on `adopt-method` is a sequencing fact of an unmerged adoption, not a request to hold a snapshot back.
- **Why:** For the actor — a developer who wants slices to reach `main` with the gate trusted — (a) is the only option under which S00's gate measures S00: on `adopt-method` `check-slice-scope` reports *not a `slice/<id>` branch — nothing to hold*, and `make verify` and `make -f delivery/Makefile verify` then prove what AC-S00-5 asks. (b) fails `check-slice-scope` with 319 findings that are the adoption's files, and repairing that in the gate is on the catastrophic list (`cruise.md`, *A failing gate is never repaired in the gate*). (c) is two things a person owns — merging the adoption (`adoption.md` 110) and a push to `main`, which D1 reserves for `make release` and which CI turns into a published snapshot (`AGENTS.md` *Versioning*). (d) moves a branch the brief did not delegate (`delegated-agent-safety.md`, last bullet) and buys nothing: run read-only against this checkout, `Scope.owning_app()` returns `None` for every path because the deployable's recorded path is `.` and the test is `path == "." or path.startswith("./")`, so `tests/test_cruise.py`, `scripts/agents/cruise.py` and `delivery/survey/running.md` are each a violation on *any* `slice/<id>` branch here, whatever its base. The ladder's branch shape is therefore not available in this repository today for two independent reasons — the unmerged adoption, which a person removes, and `check-slice-scope.py`'s root-path case, which is the factory's (`delivery/.written` line 75; a fix lands under `assets/` and reaches here through a person's `slipwai migrate`, D9). The second is a finding for the host to place against `story-split.md` (D13). Constitution X is not in force, so nothing here contradicts a MUST; principle V's green-before-push holds trivially, and the gates still run on every increment.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person merges the adoption to `main` (or pushes `adopt-method` and says in writing that `slice/<id>` branches may be cut from and merged into it) — the next slice then follows the ladder's full shape: claim on the forge, worktree, demo unpushed, push, merge into `main` in split order, Phase 4 on `main`; `check-slice-scope` going green on those branches additionally needs the root-path case fixed in the factory's checker (D13), a separate precondition the host tracks, not a condition of this entry.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S00-run-path/plan.md` (*Branch and integration* section); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/decisions.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D13 — Where does the slice-scope checker's root-path defect go?
- **Stage:** plan · **Slice:** S00-run-path (found while deciding D12) · **When:** 2026-10-03T02:58:00Z · **Iteration:** 2
- **Question:** The skipper, running `delivery/scripts/check-slice-scope.py`'s `Scope` read-only, found that `owning_app()` (line 213–219) never matches a path when the deployable's recorded `path` is `.` — the test is `path == app_path or path.startswith(app_path + "/")`, and no git path is `.` or starts with `./` — so on any `slice/<id>` branch in a repository adopted at its root, every changed file is *outside every deployable* and the gate is red. The file is the factory's (`delivery/.written`); a fix lands under `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-slice-scope.py` and reaches this repository through a person's `slipwai migrate` (D9). Where does the fix go?
- **Options:** (a) fix it here under `delivery/scripts/` — on the catastrophic list (a gate changed in the tree it measures), not available; (b) fold it into `S01-gate-walks`, which touches other `check-*.py` scripts — a different capability (walk pruning) joined with an "and", which principle V forbids; (c) a slice of its own, a PATCH (the same answers, generated better), placed second in the split — right after S00 — because until it lands no `slice/<id>` branch in a root-adopted repository passes the gate, so it is a precondition of the ladder's full shape here alongside D12's reversal; recommended by the stage (drive.md: *a slice no purpose covers* goes to the split; a PATCH is its own level).
- **Decision:** (c). `S20-slice-scope-root` is added to `story-split.md`: PATCH; `depends_on: S00-run-path`; placed second in split order though numbered last; demo on a generated project adopted at `.` with a `slice/<id>` branch touching a test file, `make -f delivery/Makefile check-slice-scope` green. Its criteria are written at its own slice-gaps stage. This repository sees the fix only after a person runs `slipwai migrate` (D9), which the cruise report lists.
- **Why:** owner brief priority 3 — a change lands under `assets/` first and reaches this repository through `migrate`; the one-capability rule (constitution V, *one capability per slice*); AGENTS.md's versioning table — a fixed asset is a PATCH; D12's reversal clause names this fix as a precondition of `slice/<id>` branches passing here.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person says the defect is intended (a root deployable owns nothing) or wants it fixed inside another slice.

## D17 — How does a slice id with a slug (`S00-run-path`) meet checkers that read the id as `S00`?
- **Stage:** Phase 4 (archive) · **Slice:** S00-run-path · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:05:00Z · **Iteration:** 2
- **Question:** After the register row was written, `make -f delivery/Makefile check-decisions` failed: *no row for S00, which is done*. `delivery/scripts/check-decisions.py:184` and `delivery/scripts/agents/benchmark.py:633` read a done slice's id from the register row's first cell with `re.match(r"([A-Za-z]+\d+)\b", …)`, so `S00-run-path` parses as `S00`; the adversary log's rows are matched by `^## (\S+) · ` against that id, and `check-benchmark` looks for `slices/S00/benchmark.json` (a warning: *S00 is done but has no benchmark.json*). The split's ids all carry a slug (`S00-run-path` … `S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files`). What shape do the records take here, and where does the mismatch go?
- **Options:** (a) rename every slice id to its bare prefix (`S00`) across the split, the spec, the decisions and the slice directory — large churn in cumulative artifacts for a checker's regex, and the slug is what makes a board readable; (b) head each adversary-log row with the id the checker reads (`## S00 · <commit> · <date>`) and say in the row which slice it is; leave the register's first cell as the full id (it parses to the same `S00`); accept the `check-benchmark` warning (it never fails `verify`) until the factory fix lands; place the fix — take the first cell whole, or let the id regex carry the slug — in `S20-slice-scope-root`, which already owns *the method's checkers against a root-adopted repository with slug ids* — recommended by the stage, the smallest change that keeps the record complete and the gate honest; (c) edit the two scripts here — `delivery/scripts/`, on the catastrophic list.
- **Decision:** (b). The S00 row is headed `## S00 · 2108b81 · 2026-10-03` with the slice named in its first line; every later row is headed the same way until `S20` reaches this repository through `slipwai migrate`; `S20`'s *Includes* gains the two checkers' id reading.
- **Why:** `delivery/commands/cruise.md`, *A failing gate is never repaired in the gate*; the register row still marks the slice done for every reader (`check-decisions` counts it, the board reads it); the adversary record is complete and findable; the only cost is a warning that names a missing `slices/S00/benchmark.json`, which the slice's `slices/S00-run-path/benchmark.json` is.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person prefers bare ids for this feature — then the rename is one commit on the cumulative artifacts, done before the next slice starts.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/adversary-log.md`; `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S20's Includes)
- **Status:** standing

## D18 — In a repository adopted at its root, which paths stay the host's on a slice branch, and how does the checker know them?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S20-slice-scope-root · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:05:42Z · **Iteration:** 3
- **Question:** `check-slice-scope.py` is to treat a deployable recorded at `path: "."` as owning paths on a `slice/<id>` branch (D12, D13). The split's second example keeps `Makefile` a violation, so "the root deployable owns everything not under another deployable" cannot be the whole rule. Which paths stay the host's, how does the checker know them, whose are the root manifest and lock, and which deployable owns a path under a subdirectory deployable?
- **Options:** (a) the root deployable owns everything not under another deployable — not available: it contradicts the split's `Makefile` example and opens `delivery/scripts/`, `project.json`, CI and `.specify/` to a slice; (b) a fixed list of host paths in the checker; (c) the list read from `<delivery>/.written`, plus the delivery directory, plus the names the factory does not list there — recommended by the stage if `.written` is reliably present; (d) something else.
- **Decision:** (c), refined: the fixed list is the floor and `.written` only ever adds to it. **Ownership:** a deployable whose recorded path is `.` or `./` is the fallback owner — `owning_app()` tries every other deployable first, exactly as today and in today's order, and only a path none of them claims falls to the root deployable. **Host surface under a root deployable**, refused with today's *outside every deployable … the host's* message, checked only where the root deployable would otherwise claim the path: (1) everything under the delivery directory that `violation()` has not already answered (model, canvas, mockups, a new ADR and the docs keep their present answers); (2) `project.json`; (3) the root `Makefile`; (4) `.specify/`; (5) CI configuration — `.github/`, `.gitea/`, `.forgejo/`, `.gitlab/`, `.gitlab-ci.yml`, and `ci.gate` from `project.json` where it is a string; (6) harness projections and guidance — `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/`, `.codex/`, `.cursor/`, `.gemini/`, `.opencode/`; (7) every path listed in `<delivery>/.written`, where that file exists. **One exception inside the delivery directory:** `<delivery>/survey/pinned.md` and `<delivery>/survey/running.md` are the slice's — the ladder has the slice write them; the other survey pages stay the host's. **Manifest and lock:** the root deployable's own `pyproject.toml`, `uv.lock`, `package.json`, lockfiles and `requirements*.txt` are the slice's, as a manifest inside `apps/<service>/` is today; they are simply not on the host list. **Root plus subdirectory deployable:** the subdirectory deployable owns everything under its path, the root deployable the remainder, and the existing other-service rule applies unchanged to whichever owns the path. **Unchanged:** a project with no deployable at `.` reaches none of this and gets today's answers; a deployable with an empty or missing `path` still owns nothing; no new setting; PATCH. `tools/` is left off the list (in a root-adopted repository it is as likely the repository's own code; the runner still parks an iteration that changes it).
- **Why:** A developer who adopted at the root has a repository that is all one application — `src/`, `tests/`, their own `scripts/`, `docs/`, `changelog.d/`, `VERSION` — and a slice must be able to write those or no slice branch ever passes; what they must still trust is that a slice cannot rewrite the gate, the record or the run's settings. `.written` alone is the wrong source: it is always written at adoption (`src/slipwai/project/adopted.py`) but lists what `migrate` replaces, not what the host owns — a person takes a file over by deleting its line (`src/slipwai/resurvey.py`), which would open the CI gate workflow to a slice; it never lists the root `Makefile`, `.claude/settings.json` or `AGENTS.md`, and nothing under `delivery/survey/` or `delivery/baseline.json`. As an addition it can only refuse more. The fixed names are what the checker's docstring and `drive.md`'s shared-surface rule already call the host's. The survey exception is forced: `drive.md` stage 7 makes every slice that changes pre-existing code add its row to `survey/pinned.md`. Not a loosening of what the merge root or CI checks (owner priority 1): no check is removed, and a gate that today refuses every file in such a repository (D13) starts refusing the right ones. The PRD artifact was not consulted (it cannot be opened from here).
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person says a root-adopted repository's slice may not touch its own manifest or lock, or wants an allow-list (named source and test directories) instead of a named host surface — which would be a new `project.json` key and a MINOR.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S20-1 to AC-S20-9); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot: the pinned page where the adoption is not at the root)
- **Status:** standing

## D19 — Once the checkers read a register id whole, what happens to records already written under the bare prefix?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S20-slice-scope-root · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:05:42Z · **Iteration:** 3
- **Question:** D17 placed in S20 the fix that makes `check-decisions.py` and `agents/benchmark.py` read a done slice's id as the register's whole first cell. This repository, by D17, heads its adversary rows `## S00 · …` under a register cell `S00-run-path`, and another project may keep `slices/S00/` beside a register cell with a slug. Read whole, both would turn red after `slipwai migrate`. What does the fix accept?
- **Options:** (a) the whole id only — every record written under the old reading fails until a person renames it, which a PATCH may not ask; (b) the whole id first, and the bare prefix (`[A-Za-z]+\d+`, today's reading) wherever the whole id finds nothing: an adversary row headed with either satisfies the done slice, and `check-benchmark` looks for `slices/<whole id>/` then `slices/<prefix>/` — recommended by the stage, since AGENTS.md's table defines a PATCH as the same answers generated better; (c) a setting choosing the reading — a new setting for a checker's regex (owner brief, *Taste*).
- **Decision:** (b). A cell that carries no id — the header row, the separator — is still skipped; an id with no slug (`S12`) reads exactly as today.
- **Why:** AGENTS.md, *Versioning*: PATCH is the same answers, generated better, and a fragment says what a change asks of a repository already generated — here, nothing. D17's rows in this repository stay green through the migrate that brings the fix.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person wants the bare prefix retired — then a MINOR with a catch-up note naming the rows to rename.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S20-10 to AC-S20-12)
- **Status:** standing

## D20 — Does D18's fixed host list stand at a root deployable, is it extended, and from what source?
- **Stage:** convergence · **Slice:** S20-slice-scope-root · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:38:23Z · **Iteration:** 3
- **Question:** D18 fixed the host surface a slice may not write in a repository adopted at its root. Converge pass 1 (T008) found the implemented list is exactly D18's and that a slice branch may change `.mcp.json`, `opencode.json`, `GEMINI.md`, a file under `.agents/skills/`, `.kiro/settings/mcp.json`, `.circleci/config.yml`, `Jenkinsfile`, `.githooks/pre-commit`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.gitignore` and `GNUmakefile` with the gate green. Does the list stand, is it extended, and from what source?
- **Options:** (a) keep D18's list; the merge root's full gate and review catch misuse — the stage leans against this for harness hook and settings files; (b) extend the fixed floor by name in the checker; (c) read harness paths from the `registry.json` that ships beside the checker, keep fixed names for what the registry does not know, and fall back to the fixed names where the registry is absent or unreadable; (d) something else. The stage made no recommendation between (b) and (c).
- **Decision:** (c) for harness files, (b) for CI and the Makefile spellings, (a) for git hooks and `.gitignore`. D18 is extended, not overridden: its fixed names remain the floor, and everything here only adds refusals, checked only where the root deployable would otherwise claim the path. **Harness files:** the checker reads `<delivery>/scripts/agents/registry.json` and takes, from every harness row whether installed or not, `contextFile`, `skillsDir`, `commandsDir`, `agentFile.dir`, `hooks.projection.where` and `projectMcp.file`; a path starting with `~` or `/` is skipped; a path of more than one segment makes its whole first segment the host's, the way D18 holds `.claude/` whole; a single-segment path is the host's as that file; a registry that is absent, unreadable, not JSON or has a row of the wrong shape adds nothing — D18's names still answer and the checker never exits on a traceback. **CI beyond the four forges, by name:** `Jenkinsfile`, `azure-pipelines.yml`, `bitbucket-pipelines.yml`, `.circleci/`, `.woodpecker.yml`, `.drone.yml`, `.travis.yml` — what `CI_FORGES` in `src/slipwai/delivery_facts.py` recognises; `ci.evidence` is not read (it is sometimes a sentence). **Makefile spellings, by name:** `GNUmakefile` and `makefile` beside `Makefile`. **Git hooks** (`.githooks/`, `.pre-commit-config.yaml`, `.husky/`) and **`.gitignore`** are the repository's own and stay off the floor. PATCH: no setting, no new file in a project; a project with its deployables under `apps/` is unchanged.
- **Why:** A developer who adopted at the root needs their slices able to write their own application and no slice able to rewrite what gates or steers the agent doing the work; the rule that separates the families is whether the factory itself reads, writes or records the file. `.mcp.json` and `opencode.json` decide which tools the agent has and `GEMINI.md` is another agent's `AGENTS.md`; the registry already ships beside the checker and is already the source for `check-speckit.py`, the generated `.gitignore` and the runner's controls, and a hand-copied list would drift at the next registry update (owner brief, *Taste*). A repository whose pipeline is a `Jenkinsfile` deserves the answer one on GitHub gets, and `adopt` already recognises these names. GNU make reads `GNUmakefile`, then `makefile`, then `Makefile`, so a slice that adds one replaces the file D18 protects without touching it. The factory installs, reads and records no git hook, so any list would be a guess at other people's tools, and the gate is `make verify` and CI; a slice that adds a build output must be able to ignore it. Nothing loosens what the merge root or CI checks (owner priority 1): the same check refuses more names. The PRD artifact was not consulted (it cannot be opened from here).
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person with a root-adopted repository says its own work routinely edits a registry-named path that is not agent configuration — then the registry reading narrows to the installed harnesses or the exact paths — or says its git hooks are part of the gate it trusts, in which case they join the floor by name.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S20-2 amended; AC-S20-14 to AC-S20-16); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S20-slice-scope-root/tasks.md` (T008); `changelog.d/slice-scope-root-deployable.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D21 — Do the migration rule and the events rule of `check-slice-scope` hold for the root deployable of an adopted repository?
- **Stage:** gaps (after converge) · **Slice:** S20-slice-scope-root · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:52:58Z · **Iteration:** 3
- **Question:** With S20 a deployable at `.` owns its own code on a `slice/<id>` branch (D18, D20). Two rules written for the layout the factory generates, unreachable at a root adoption before S20, now answer for an adopted application's own code: the migration rule refuses a new `shop/migrations/0002_add_field.py` (Django's numbering) and a new `db/migrations/20261003120000_add.js` (a 14-digit stamp) because it wants exactly 12 digits, and refuses an edited existing migration; the events rule refuses a removed line in a pre-existing file under a `domain` directory whose stem ends in `events`. Do the two rules hold there, and if not, what does?
- **Options:** (a) both rules hold as they are, explained in the docstring and the fragment; (b) neither applies where the owner is the root deployable or one the record marks as not generated; (c) a middle: keep "an existing migration is never edited or deleted", accept the ecosystem's own names for new migrations, drop the events rule there; (d) out of S20's scope: leave the code, state the limitation, place a slice. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (c), keyed on the root deployable, with (d) for an adopted deployable under a subdirectory. It applies only where the path's owner is the deployable recorded at `.` and the path is not on the host surface. **New migration:** accepted under whatever name the repository's own tool wrote — every name `MIGRATION_NAME` matches, with no demand on the digit count. **Existing migration:** an edit or a deletion is still refused there, and the message says to add a new migration with the repository's own tool, not a timestamped one. **Events rule:** does not apply at the root deployable unless its record declares `"layout": "hexagonal"`; where it does, the rule holds as today. **Unchanged:** a deployable under a subdirectory, generated or recorded `"generated": false`, keeps today's answers for both rules; so does a migration-shaped path on the host surface, and every project with no deployable at `.`. No setting; PATCH.
- **Why:** A developer who adopted a Django, Rails or knex application at the root gets the migration name their framework writes, and a gate that refuses it stalls every slice with a schema change on a rule their code never had (`AGENTS.md`, *Delivery method*: existing code is held to nothing it did not have before). The 12-digit stamp guards the factory's own lexical runner; at an adopted root the runner is the framework's, and a collision surfaces without the checker (an add/add conflict, or Django refusing two leaves). The edit refusal stays because the release running against the database already applied the file. The events rule reads a directory name and a file stem, and in an adopted application `domain/events.py` may be anything; `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-imports.py` already passes over an application that was here unless it declares `layout: hexagonal`, and the same declaration is the opt-in here. The key is the root and not `generated: false` because lifting the rules at a subdirectory deployable would remove a refusal every released version makes — the owner brief's *Always ask a person*. Nothing loosens at the merge root or in CI: the checker holds nothing off a slice branch, and at a root deployable every one of these paths was refused wholesale before S20. The PRD artifact was not consulted (it cannot be opened from here).
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person with a root-adopted repository says its migrations are applied in plain lexical order by a runner with no conflict detection of its own — then the stamp demand returns there, widened to 12 or more digits.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S20-17 to AC-S20-19); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S20-slice-scope-root/tasks.md` (T013); `changelog.d/slice-scope-root-deployable.md`
- **Status:** standing

- **Stage:** gaps (after converge) · **Slice:** S20-slice-scope-root · **When:** 2026-10-03T05:52:58Z · **Iteration:** 3
- **Question:** T010 (delivery at the root with a root deployable leaves `skills/`, `commands/` writable), T011 (a `.written` that is not UTF-8, or a pathologically nested registry, ends the checker on a traceback), T012 (files the registry names only in prose are not the host's; a row naming `src/mcp.json` would make `src/` the host's), and the gaps pass's G2 (`specs/cruise-log.jsonl` refused on a slice branch), G3 (two deployables at `.`: the first listed owns everything), G4 (the docstring's *Refused* paragraph contradicts its root bullet), G5 (a `.written` line with trailing whitespace), G6 (one bare-prefix row satisfies every done slice sharing the prefix). - **Why:** owner brief, *Taste* (a gate says what it checked in one line a person can act on — never a traceback) and priority 2 (scope before cleverness); constitution V's one capability per slice keeps G2 out; D18 and D20 are the standing readings T010 and T012 restate.

## D24 — Which files does a refresh leave to the project: the two that were seen reset, or every settings file the factory seeds?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files · **When:** 2026-10-03T06:51:34Z · **Iteration:** 4
- **Question:** D15 placed in S21 that `slipwai adopt --refresh` never rewrites `.specify/cruise.json` or `.specify/product-owner.md`. The loop that reset them (`src/slipwai/resurvey.py`, *rewritten wherever the disk differs from what the record now drives*) treats `.specify/models.json` and `.specify/drive.json` the same way: all four come from `scaffold.project_files` with content no fact in `project.json` drives, and all four are changed by the project — through `/cruise-settings`, `/model-delegation-settings`, `/drive-settings`, or by hand. On this repository the other two were not reset only because nobody had changed them. Does the slice close the two, or the four; what happens where one is missing, uncommitted, or in `.written`?
- **Options:** (a) the two files D15 named, the others left for a later slice — the next person to change a model table meets the same reset; (b) the four, as one rule: a file the factory seeds and the project then owns is written by a refresh only where it is absent — recommended by the stage (drive.md, *each appended task closes the class, not the instance*; the generated text already says whose they are: `drive.md` *the table is the project owner's to change at any point*, `cruise.md` *settings change only through /cruise-settings*, the brief's own first lines); (c) every file a person might edit, `.specify/extensions.yml` and the presets included — those are driven by the record (the adoption's hooks, the journey template follow the map), so leaving them would stop the pages following the record, which is the refresh's one job.
- **Decision:** (b). The four are written by a refresh only where absent — then with the factory's default, so `make check-agents` and `/cruise` never find one missing. Present, they are never compared, rewritten or counted; an uncommitted change to one no longer refuses the refresh, since the refusal protects only what a run writes (`src/slipwai/uncommitted.py`). They stay listed in `.written`: `slipwai migrate` is a three-way merge and is how a newer factory's default reaches them beside the project's change; the `owned:` line keeps its meaning (a line a person deleted). `adopt --confirm` runs the same refresh and gets the same rule. `generate`, `add-service` and `migrate` are untouched. No report line is added for a file left alone: nothing happened to it.
- **Why:** The actor cruises a repository on settings they chose; a survey that silently turns the run off, or swaps the model table back, costs them an iteration before they notice — and the owner brief is the one page the skipper decides from. Owner brief priority 3: the fix lands in the factory and reaches projects through `migrate`. PATCH: the same answers, generated better; experimental (brownfield adoption), so the fragment says so.
- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D15)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person says a refresh is meant to bring the factory's current defaults for `models.json` or `drive.json` — then those two return to the loop and the slice documents that a refresh resets them.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S21-1 to AC-S21-8)
- **Status:** standing

## D25 — From which rows does a refresh derive `strategy.before`, and for which preconditions?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files · **When:** 2026-10-03T06:51:34Z · **Iteration:** 4
- **Question:** D16 placed in S21 that a refresh re-derives `strategy.before` so the strategy page stops naming a prerequisite the map says is met. `with_recommendation()` (`src/slipwai/strategy.py`) computes `before` from `detected()` — the tree's reading — before the rows are reconciled with what a person recorded, so a row `confirmed` at `tests-pass` still yields *the safety net is `tests-exist`*. Which rows does `before` follow, and is it the safety net alone?
- **Options:** (a) the safety-net sentence only — the instance that was seen; (b) every precondition `preconditions()` derives from a row (path to production, safety net, structure), read from the rows as the map shows them after the refresh — reconciled, a person's `confirmed` or `overridden` rung included — recommended by the stage (the same class rule; the map's page and the strategy page are generated from one record and must not disagree); (c) also re-derive `recommended`, `because` and the programme from reconciled rows — none of them reads a rung today, so there is nothing to follow, and changing how a strategy is recommended is another capability.
- **Decision:** (b). The platform entries at the head of `before`, and `recommended`, `because`, `decided`, `finished`, `programme`, are derived as today.
- **Why:** A developer reading `change-strategy.md` is told what to do before the strategy is worth starting; a line the map already shows as done sends them to do it again or to distrust the page. The rule is the refresh's own: a person's recorded answer stands over the tree's reading.
- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D16)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person wants `before` to stay the tree's reading as a second opinion beside the map — then the page says so in words instead.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S21-9, AC-S21-10)
- **Status:** standing

## D26 — Does the owner brief's own line, *`slipwai migrate` never rewrites it*, contradict D24, and is the generated text changed?
- **Stage:** convergence (pass 1, T008) · **Slice:** S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files · **When:** 2026-10-03T07:21:46Z · **Iteration:** 4
- **Question:** `src/slipwai/project/decisions.py` writes into every `.specify/product-owner.md`: *This file is human-owned. `/cruise` reads it and never writes it; `slipwai migrate` never rewrites it.* D24, AC-S21-8 and the fragment say the brief stays in `.written` so that `migrate` merges a newer factory's version with the project's. The converge pass asked which statement is wrong, and whether the brief is meant to be exempt from the merge.
- **Options:** (a) the two agree and nothing changes: `migrate` is a three-way merge of the factory's change with the project's text — it never replaces the brief with the factory's version, which is what *rewrite* means and what the refresh did — recommended by the stage's own first step (*see which of the two a migrate actually does*): `src/slipwai/migrate.py` and `replay.py` carry no exemption for the brief and no path that writes a listed file over a project's change; (b) reword the generated line to say *merges* — a change to the template of a file every project has filled in, which hands each of them a merge on their own brief for one word; (c) exempt the brief from `migrate` — a change to what `migrate` writes, which S21 defers and D24 decided against.
- **Decision:** (a). The line stands as written; no generated text changes; T008 is closed as *stated, not changed*.

- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D24)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a `migrate` over a filled-in brief is ever seen to conflict or replace a section — then the line is reworded, or the brief leaves the merge, as its own PATCH.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files/tasks.md` (T008)

## D27 — What do the after-converge gaps findings on S21 become: tests, a stated reading, or a line for the audit?
- **Stage:** gaps (after converge) · **Slice:** S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files · **When:** 2026-10-03T07:28:41Z · **Iteration:** 4
- **Question:** The gaps pass (`drive-gaps`, read-only, clone at `e8af5a2`) found no CRITICAL or HIGH and no promise the code does not keep. ## D28 — When a person lowers `release.path` under a Path to production row they placed, which one does a refresh follow?
- **Stage:** Phase 4 (adversary triage) · **Slice:** S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files · **When:** 2026-10-03T07:47:42Z · **Iteration:** 4
- **Question:** After D25, `strategy.before` reads the reconciled rows. The adversary (seam B, F1, MEDIUM, a regression) showed that `slipwai adopt --yes --release pipeline` writes the row `pipeline` / `overridden`; a person who later sets `release.path` to `manual` and refreshes keeps the old row, because `reconciled()` lets a person's row stand. `strategy.before` and `change-strategy.md` then drop *a pipeline that deploys on a passing `verify`* — a prerequisite shown as met when it is not, where the page was right before the slice. How is the regression fixed?
- **Options:** (a) the row follows the person's newer answer wherever the fresh row carries a person's provenance and differs — named by the adversary; (b) narrow D25 for this axis: `before` reads the fresh reading where the `release` record is a person's, and the map stays stale — named by the adversary; (c) drop an entry only where neither reading says it is unmet — rejected: it puts back D16's stale sentence (tree `tests-exist`, person `tests-pass`); (d) something else. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (a), narrowed to the direction the gate already decides. In `reconciled()` (`src/slipwai/convergence.py`), a person's Path to production row no longer stands when all three hold: the freshly derived row carries a person's provenance (`confirmed` or `overridden`), which `detected()` gives it only from a `release` record a person answered; its rung is `unknown`, `manual` or `scripted`; and the recorded rung is above it on the ladder. The row then takes the fresh rung, evidence and provenance, and keeps its `planned`. The refresh says so in one line of the standing shape — *convergence: path-to-production refreshed from `pipeline` to `manual`* — naming `release.path`. The map, `strategy.before` and `change-strategy.md` all follow from that one row; `before_of()` and `with_reconciled()` are not special-cased; D25 stands and AC-S21-10 keeps its text. **Stands as today:** a person's row at or below what the record says (the mirror: adopted `--release manual`, later `release.path: pipeline` — the row stays `manual` and the entry is still printed); a row at `one-path` or `pipeline-decides` over `release.path: pipeline`; a person's row over a `release` record that is `detected` or `unrecorded`; every other axis. **Parking Lot:** a person's row does not follow a person's `release`, `database` or `infrastructure` record upward or sideways, though `ground.md` says *the row follows when `/survey` re-reads the record* — older than S21, not a regression. S21's *Includes* gains this clause; PATCH, experimental; the slice's one fragment gains a sentence; nothing is asked of a repository already adopted. The criterion is AC-S21-12; the failing test adopts `node_repository` with `--release pipeline`, sets `release.path` to `manual`, commits, refreshes, and expects the row at `manual` / `overridden`, the report line, and the entry on both the record and the page — its RED, seen at the slice's tip: the row is still `pipeline` and the entry absent from both.
- **Why:** A developer who records that the pipeline was dropped, where `ground.md` tells them to (*`release.path` in `project.json` … the row follows*), today gets three bad results from one refresh: a map that still says `pipeline`, a strategy page that calls the pipeline done, and a red gate — in a temp-directory run `make -f delivery/Makefile check-convergence` exited 1 with *path-to-production is recorded at `pipeline` (overridden), but release.path is `manual` — the tree allows `manual` at most*. So the winner in this direction is not a new judgement: the gate and `ground.md` (*the tree wins*) already say the row cannot stand, and the refresh now writes what the gate would have made the person write by hand. (b) puts the page right by making it disagree with the map on purpose — the disagreement D25 exists to end — and leaves the gate red. (a) as first written would pull a person's `one-path` back to `pipeline` on every refresh, since `release.path` cannot spell that rung, and in the mirror case would choose between two answers a person gave with nothing saying which is newer. Constitution V's one capability holds — this is the D16/D25 capability with its regression fixed at the cause; constitution I holds; nothing changes what the merge root or CI checks. The PRD artifact was not consulted. Not an ADR: reversing it is a refactor of one branch and its test.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person says a row they placed by hand must outrank their own `release` record even where `check-convergence` fails it — the refresh would then leave the row and print the contradiction as a `disagrees:` line, and `before` would take the lower of the two readings for this axis.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S21-12); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S21's Includes; Parking Lot); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S21-refresh-keeps-owned-files/tasks.md` (T010); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/adversary-log.md` (F1); `changelog.d/refresh-keeps-owned-files.md`
- **Status:** standing
