## D30 — Which name is the trunk a slice branch is compared with, and who may say so?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T08:38:47Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** The split says the base is where the branch left the trunk `project.json` records (`ci.branch`, else `main`), with `master` counting only where no `main` exists. Two things are unsettled. First, which commit's `project.json` names the trunk: a slice that commits `ci.branch: <a branch at its own head>` makes the base HEAD, so the diff is empty and the edit is never seen. Second, the mirror of the `master` rule: in a `master` repository a minted `main` empties the diff. Also to settle: what an unusable `ci.branch` value does, and what happens where the recorded trunk has no ref while `main` does.
- **Options:** (a) the working tree's name, accepted as is; (b) two-step — find the base with the working tree's name, read `ci.branch` at that base, refuse on a difference; (c) every name any reading offers, oldest base across names, newest within a name; (d) anchor on what a branch's content cannot change — `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`, or the pull request's target in CI — with the record as fallback; (e) a combination. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (e), built from (d)'s pull-request anchor and (c)'s oldest-across-names, with the record as the working tree has it. **Names:** a name is usable when it is a non-empty string that is a valid branch name once a leading `refs/heads/` is taken off, and it is not of the shape `slice/<id>`; anything else — not a string, empty, whitespace, a leading `-`, another `refs/` prefix, a slice branch's name — records nothing: it adds no candidate, ends nothing on a traceback, and is not itself a failure (D22's tolerant reading). **Refs:** a name is looked up only as `refs/heads/<name>` and `refs/remotes/origin/<name>`, by full name, so a tag never answers; within one name the newest base of the two wins, as today (the split's third example). **The recorded trunk:** `ci.branch` from the working tree's `project.json`, where usable and where it has a ref; otherwise `main` where `main` has a ref, and otherwise `master` — so `master` counts only where it is the recorded name or where no `main` exists, and a recorded name with no ref in the checkout falls through to `main` exactly as an absent record does, which is today's answer. **The forge's word:** where CI names the pull request's target (`GITHUB_BASE_REF`, `CI_MERGE_REQUEST_TARGET_BRANCH_NAME`) and that name is usable and has a ref, it is a second candidate, and across two different names the **oldest** base wins — the target can only move the base back, never forward. **The report line:** the pass line and the refusal header name the trunk compared with and the base's short commit; where a recorded `ci.branch` was passed over (unusable, or no ref), the line says so and gives the fetch that would bring it. **Not taken:** (a) alone, and (b) — where the minted branch sits at HEAD the base's record is the branch's own, so (b) closes nothing; `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` — it goes stale at a rename, and `adopt` already wrote it into `ci.branch`; unconditional oldest-across-`main`-and-`master` — a repository that renamed its trunk and kept the old branch is green today and would turn red, which a PATCH may not ask (D19). **What is promised where:** *locally*, nothing a slice branch commits, and no stray `master`, `origin/master` or tag named `main`, moves the base forward; a person who moves refs in their own checkout can defeat it, as they can by moving `main` itself, and that is not promised against. *In CI on a pull request against the trunk*, nothing the branch commits or pushes moves the base forward — given a base to compare with at all, which is D31's. **Stated residual:** a push pipeline with no pull-request target has only the local promise; a pull request aimed at a branch other than the trunk is held only as far as the record reaches, and it merges nothing into the trunk. **Depends on D31 only for this:** where no candidate has a ref there is no base, and what the checker then does is D31's; this entry supplies the name its message tells a person to fetch — the pull request's target where CI gives one, else the recorded trunk, else `main`. **Parking Lot:** reading the forge's default branch on push pipelines (`CI_DEFAULT_BRANCH`, the event payload) is another anchor and another capability; the refresh that writes the checked-out branch into `ci.branch` is already there and stays there — this rule only stops it emptying the gate.
- **Why:** A developer trusts this gate to tell them a slice stayed in its lane; a gate that passes because it compared the branch with itself is worse than none. The dangerous door needs no hostility and no minted ref: the Parking Lot already records that a refresh off the trunk writes the checked-out branch into `ci.branch`; run on `slice/S1`, that commits `ci.branch: slice/S1`, and a checker that takes the working tree's name as is would compare the branch with itself for the rest of its life and never show the `project.json` edit — refusing a slice-shaped name as a trunk closes exactly that. Every other name a branch can write is already safe: it either has no ref (falls to `main`, today's answer) or names a branch that does not contain the edit, so the base sits before the edit and the existing rule refuses `project.json` as the host's; what remains needs a ref created at or after the edit. Locally that is not defensible — a local ref-minter can as easily move `main` — so no local rule is worth an honest project's green. In CI the forge's word closes it: a branch can push `evil`, or a `main` beside a `master` trunk, but it cannot change what the pull request targets, and counting the target under oldest-wins closes both doors there; because the target only moves the base back, a stacked pull request keeps today's answer and nothing loosens at CI (owner priority 1). Honest projects keep or gain: a generated project records no `ci` block (only `adopt` writes one, `src/slipwai/adopt.py:185`) and its workflow is hardcoded to `main` (`src/slipwai/project/ci_workflows.py`), so it resolves to `main` as today; a repository adopted on `master` resolves to `master`; a renamed trunk with a stale `master` stays green; a trunk named neither `main` nor `master` is compared with its own trunk for the first time — today it has no base and the checker holds nothing. Slice branches reach generated and adopted CI only through `pull_request` (`ci_workflows.py`, `src/slipwai/project/adopted_ci.py`), so the target variable is there whenever CI checks one; the meaning of the two variables is from the skipper's memory of the forges, not from this tree, and no reproduction was run. The PRD artifact was not consulted. PATCH: no setting, no new file, and the changed pass line is the Taste rule that a gate says what it checked. Not an ADR: reversing it is a refactor of `merge_base()` and its tests.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person shows an honest repository where the pull request's target and the recorded trunk are different long-lived branches and the slice is rightly compared with the newer — oldest-across-names would then charge it with the other branch's files; the target would become the base outright on a pull request, and that is a person's call because it changes what CI checks.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-1 to AC-S22-13); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S22's *Includes*; Parking Lot: the forge's default branch on push pipelines); `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-slice-scope.py` (module docstring, at implementation)
- **Status:** standing

## D31 — On a `slice/<id>` branch with no usable base, what does `check-slice-scope` answer — locally, and in CI where every pull-request checkout is depth 1 with no trunk ref?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T08:35:44Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** The split's second example for S22 makes a shallow clone with no trunk ref a failure "saying what to fetch". Every CI run of a slice pull request in a generated or adopted project is such a checkout (`actions/checkout@v6` with no `fetch-depth`; `fetch --depth 1` in the container jobs), so the CI half of the gate has never held anything. Taken literally with the workflows unchanged, every slice pull request turns red after `slipwai migrate`. What does S22 do there, what does it do in the neighbouring cases, and is `check-migrations.py`'s identical base selection swept in?
- **Options:** (a) fail everywhere and change the generated `verify` job's checkout and the adopted delivery workflow to fetch history; (b) fail locally, keep exit 0 in CI with a line that says nothing was held, no workflow change, the workflow change a slice of its own or a person's decision; (c) fail everywhere, change no workflow; (d) local behaviour only, the CI question `unavailable: a person's approval`; (e) something else, such as splitting on `--is-shallow-repository`. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (b), with the workflow change placed as a slice of its own whose go-ahead is **`unavailable: a person's approval`**. S22 changes only `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-slice-scope.py` and its tests. The rule has one question — *is there a commit this branch can be compared with?* — and four answers. **1. A trunk ref exists and a merge base is found:** held against it, as D30 selects it. **2. The checkout is the developer's (the branch name came from `git`, not from a forge variable) and there is no usable base:** exit 1, one line, whatever the cause — no trunk ref, in a full clone or a shallow one: *slice/<id> has no `<trunk>` to compare with, so nothing can be held — run `git fetch origin <trunk>`*; trunk ref present, no common ancestor, `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` true: *shares no history with `<trunk>` at this depth — run `git fetch --unshallow origin`*; trunk ref present, no common ancestor, not shallow: *shares no history with `<trunk>` — a slice branch is cut from `<trunk>`*. A brand-new repository whose first branch is a slice branch fails the same way: a slice is cut from the trunk, and "no trunk" is not a state in which a slice's reach can be judged. `<trunk>` is whatever D30 names. **3. The branch name came from `GITHUB_HEAD_REF` or `CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME` and `HEAD` is detached (a forge's pull-request checkout) and there is no usable base:** exit 0, as today, but the words *nothing to hold* go; the line goes to stderr and says the slice *was NOT checked — this CI checkout has no `<trunk>` history to compare with*, that the check holds on a developer's machine, and that for it to hold here the verify job's checkout needs `fetch-depth: 0` (on GitLab, `GIT_DEPTH: "0"`). Where the variable names the branch but `HEAD` is attached to a branch, the checkout is a developer's and answer 2 applies, so the variable alone cannot buy the exit 0. **4. In CI with a usable base** (a project that already fetches history): held exactly as locally. **The CI half stays open and is said so:** A3 in the adversary log is closed by S22 for the minted base and the local shallow clone; its CI half is recorded open as slice `S24-ci-fetches-slice-base` — the generated `verify` job and the adopted delivery workflow (and its GitLab job) fetch the history `check-slice-scope` needs, and the checker then fails in CI where there is still none — placed after `S23`, a PATCH, `depends_on: S22-slice-scope-base`, its row carrying *needs a person's approval before it is driven* (owner brief, *Always ask a person*, first item); the cruise report lists it beside E8. The split's second example for S22 is amended to *a developer's shallow clone*. **`check-migrations.py`:** left alone in S22 and placed in the Parking Lot with `assets/targets/*/scripts/check-flags.py`; their written stance (*says nothing false on a shallow one*) stands, and the same mintable `master` / tag-named-`main` base in their own base selection is recorded there for the completion audit.
- **Why:** For the developer, the local half is plain: a gate that prints *nothing to hold* and passes when it could not look is the exact thing A3 found, and on their own machine the fix is one `git fetch` the line hands them. The CI half is different in kind. `src/slipwai/project/ci_workflows.py`'s `verify` job and `src/slipwai/project/adopted_ci.py`'s `delivery_workflow` both use `actions/checkout@v6` with no `fetch-depth`, which on a pull request leaves a detached merge commit at depth 1 with no `origin/<trunk>` under `refs/` — a known property of the action, read and not run — so `merge_base()` returns `None` and `check()` exits 0; D23's premise that "a pushed `master` is fetched there" does not hold. (c) is rejected: it turns every slice pull request red in a PATCH for a reason the branch cannot fix. (a) is rejected for this slice: it is two capabilities — how the base is chosen and how CI gets its code — which constitution V forbids, and it makes CI start refusing pull requests it passes today; that is tightening, and probably what priority 1 wants, but the owner brief says *anything that changes what … CI checks* is a person's, and a skipper's reading of what the owner would want is not that approval. Nothing loosens: CI's exit is today's, and the line stops claiming there was nothing to hold and says it did not check and which workflow key fixes it — as close to "never a false green" as the slice can come without the workflow. On speed: `fetch-depth: 0` has precedent in `assets/toolkit/scripts/extensions/ux-gates/init.py` and costs seconds against minutes of toolchain setup on the one `verify` job; speed is not the reason for deferring, the approval is. `check-migrations` is a different promise — it tells *new in this change* from old, and its docstring already chooses silence on a shallow checkout — and S22's row excludes "any other change to what the checker holds"; closing the class here means every no-base case of *this* checker, which the four answers do. The PRD artifact was not consulted. PATCH: a fixed asset, no new file, setting, flag or option, and CI's exit code unchanged in every project; no catch-up note is owed, since `migrate` completes the change itself. Not an ADR: reversing it is a refactor of one branch in `check()` and its tests.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person approves changing the generated and adopted checkouts — `S24-ci-fetches-slice-base` is then driven, and answer 3 becomes exit 1 with the same line in the commit that changes the workflows; if they say it belongs inside S22, this entry is overridden to (a).
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-14 to AC-S22-21); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S22's second example and *Includes*; the S24 row and graph edge; Parking Lot: the migrations and flags checkers); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/adversary-log.md` (A3's status)
- **Status:** standing

## D32 — In a CI run that is not a pull-request checkout (a push pipeline on a `slice/<id>` branch: `HEAD` attached, no branch-name variable) with no usable base, what does `check-slice-scope` answer?
- **Stage:** convergence (pass 1, T013) · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T09:08:43Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** D31 tells a forge's checkout from a developer's by *the branch name came from `GITHUB_HEAD_REF` or `CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME` and `HEAD` is detached*. The converge pass ran the shape of a GitHub/Gitea push run on a slice branch: a depth-1 single-branch clone of `slice/S1`, `HEAD` attached, neither variable, `CI=true GITHUB_ACTIONS=true`. After S22 it exits 1 with *run `git fetch origin main`*; before S22 it exited 0 with *nothing to hold*. By D31's letter that is a developer's checkout. By D31's reason for rejecting its option (c), and by the plan's *CI's exit code unchanged in every project*, a CI run should not turn red in a PATCH for a reason the branch cannot fix. Which holds?
- **Options:** (a) keep exit 1 there; say so in the fragment and docstring, and narrow the plan's constraint to *every project as generated or adopted*; (b) a checkout is the forge's wherever a CI marker is set (`CI`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITLAB_CI`), attached or not, with or without a pull-request variable, and there the no-base answer is *NOT checked*, exit 0; (c) something else. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (b), as an addition to D31's answer 3, not a replacement. **The rule:** a checkout is the forge's where either D31's test holds (name from `GITHUB_HEAD_REF` or `CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME`, and `HEAD` detached) or the environment says the run is CI: `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITLAB_CI` or `CI` non-empty — non-empty is how `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-ux-gates.py` already reads `CI`. **No base in a forge's checkout:** every no-base state answers as D31's answer 3 does — exit 0, nothing on stdout that reads as a pass, and the existing *NOT checked* line on stderr naming the trunk and `fetch-depth: 0` / `GIT_DEPTH: "0"`; it never prints `git fetch origin <trunk>`, which nobody can run on a runner. **What a marker does not buy:** with a usable base the slice is held exactly as locally (D31's answer 4); a lost record at a canonical slot still fails (AC-S22-19); a branch that is not `slice/<id>` keeps today's answer. **D31's answer 2 is narrowed by one clause:** a developer's checkout is one where the name came from git, or from a variable with `HEAD` attached, *and no CI marker is set*; AC-S22-17 keeps its exit 1 and its test, and reads *the branch-name variable alone does not buy the exit 0*. **The plan's constraint stands as written**, now citing D31 and D32. **The tests' own environment:** the suites that assert a developer's exit 1 run the checker with the three markers cleared, beside the branch variables they already clear — without that, the factory's own `verify` job turns red on its first CI run. **S24:** when a person approves it, the exit 1 it introduces covers this arm too, and its row says so. PATCH: no setting, flag or file. D31 stands with this entry beside it; D30 is unchanged.
- **Why:** The developer who widened their triggers to `slice/**` did nothing wrong. After a `slipwai migrate` that claims PATCH, their pipeline would go red with a command that cannot be run where it is printed and that no commit on the branch can satisfy — the outcome D31 rejected its option (c) for, and D19's test forbids it. It is also a person's call: CI failing where it passed is *anything that changes what … CI checks* in the owner brief's *Always ask a person*; D31 placed that change in S24 behind a person's approval, and (a) would pre-empt it for one corner. The code already disagreed with itself across forges: `gitlab_job()` in `src/slipwai/project/adopted_ci.py` writes no `rules:` and no `GIT_DEPTH`, so an adopted GitLab project runs the gate on whatever pipelines its own configuration creates, branch pipelines included; there GitLab sets `CI_COMMIT_REF_NAME` on a detached `HEAD`, so the code as built already answered *NOT checked*, exit 0, for a GitLab push pipeline on a slice branch and exit 1 for the same run on GitHub (the GitLab runner's behaviour, and Gitea setting `GITHUB_ACTIONS` as GitHub does, are from the skipper's memory of the forges; no reproduction was run). Nothing loosens (owner priority 1): before S22 every one of these runs exited 0 saying *nothing to hold*; they still exit 0, and now say they did not look and which workflow key makes them look. The cost is on a developer's machine: a shell with `CI` set, in a checkout with no trunk to compare with, gets the loud non-check instead of the failure — a marker never produces a line that reads as a pass, and it does nothing where a base exists, which is the ordinary local case; D30 already declines to promise against a person who rearranges their own checkout. `CI` and not only the two forge markers, because `adopt` recognises Jenkins, CircleCI, Woodpecker and the rest (D20), and *unchanged in every project* is not kept by naming two forges. D30's stated residual is untouched: it describes a push pipeline that has a base; this entry covers the one that has none. Not an ADR: reversing it is a refactor of `forge_checkout()` and its tests. The PRD artifact was not consulted.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person says a local checkout with `CI` set must fail rather than report a non-check — the marker list then drops to `GITHUB_ACTIONS` and `GITLAB_CI`, and other CI systems take (a)'s catch-up note; or a person approves S24, and this arm becomes exit 1 with the others in that slice.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-17 amended; AC-S22-22 to AC-S22-24); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/plan.md` (*Constraints*); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/tasks.md` (T013); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S24's row); `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-slice-scope.py` and `changelog.d/slice-scope-base.md` (at T013)
- **Status:** standing

## D33 — Where `project.json` records no trunk and both `main` and `master` have refs, which one is a slice branch compared with (gaps finding G4)?
- **Stage:** gaps (after converge), finding G4 · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T09:32:45Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** With no `ci` record, a `master` trunk one commit ahead of a stale `main`, and a slice cut from `master` that changes only its own file, the check exited 0 before the slice (`7859512`, newest base among four short names) and exits 1 at the slice's tip (*compared with `main`*, `Makefile` refused — `master`'s own file charged to the slice). This mirrors AC-S22-12, and D30 rejected oldest-across-names because a PATCH may not turn an honest green red. Which name is the trunk there, and what does the gate say?
- **Options:** (a) `main` wins where unrecorded and both exist, with a catch-up telling such a repository to record `ci.branch: master` or delete the stale `main`; (b) keep the old answer, the newest base of the two names; (c) tiebreak on `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD` where it points at `origin/main` or `origin/master`, else `main`; (d) the descendant of the two is the trunk. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (a), with the gate saying so itself. **The rule is D30's, unchanged:** unrecorded, `main` where `main` has a ref, else `master`. **What is added is words, not selection:** where `ci.branch` records nothing usable, both names have refs, and `master`'s base is strictly newer than `main`'s, the pass line and the refusal header carry one clause — *`master` is here too and `project.json` records no trunk — if `master` is the trunk, set `ci.branch` to `master` in `project.json` on it, or delete the stale `main`*. Where `master`'s base is the same or older (AC-S22-12's repository) the clause is absent and the output is today's. The clause changes no base and no exit code, locally or in CI. **Not taken:** (b) reopens AC-S22-1's first example. (d) is not available: a `master` trunk one commit past a stale `main` with a slice on top is the same commit graph as a `main` trunk with a `master` minted one commit below the slice's head, so no ancestry rule tells them apart; a refinement that only refuses a `master` exactly at `HEAD` keeps AC-S22-1's letter and loses D30's local promise. (c) is not taken: a clone made before a `master`→`main` rename keeps `origin/HEAD` on `origin/master`, so the tiebreak would turn AC-S22-12's honest green red in a far commoner repository; an ancestry guard would mend that, but the checkout that created the repository and almost every CI checkout have no `origin/HEAD`, so the same commit would be green in one checkout and red in another (owner priority 5); and the catch-up would be owed anyway. AC-S22-1, -3, -9 and -12 keep their answers; AC-S22-13 gains the clause; AC-S22-21's fragment gains one sentence. D30 stands with this entry beside it; one sentence of its *Why* — *honest projects keep or gain* — is true except for the unrecorded `master` trunk with a stale `main`, which loses its green until the trunk is recorded.
- **Why:** A developer trusts this gate to say a slice stayed in its lane. The only rule that keeps G4's repository green is one a slice branch can satisfy by creating a `master`, which is the hole the slice exists to close; that is the difference from D19 and from D30's rejected oldest-across-names — there a fallback kept the honest green at no cost to what the gate catches, here none exists. The reach is narrow and was checked in the tree: `adopt` always records a trunk (`src/slipwai/adopt.py:185`, from `default_branch()` in `src/slipwai/delivery_facts.py`; the pickaxe finds that line only in the initial public commit — no `v*` tag exists in this checkout, so "every released `adopt`" rests on that commit); `generate` records none but starts on `main` (`git init -b main` in `src/slipwai/scaffold.py`, and its workflow pushes on `branches: [main]`). So the affected repository is a generated project a person moved to `master` while keeping `main` and the workflow's trigger, or one whose `ci` block was removed by hand. For that repository the cost is real and is stated: after `migrate`, a slice branch is charged with `master`'s own files until someone records the trunk; the fix is one line in the host's `project.json`, and the gate prints it where it fails. A PATCH's fragment may say what it asks of a generated repository (`AGENTS.md`), and this slice's fragment already carries two such lines. In CI the verdict is D30's as it stands. Nothing loosens at the merge root or in CI (owner priority 1), there is no new setting, and it stays one capability. The PRD artifact was not consulted; G4's reproduction was taken from the gaps pass and from reading `merge_base()`, not re-run by the skipper. Not an ADR: reversing it removes a clause from a report line and its tests.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person shows that generated projects living on `master` beside a leftover `main` are ordinary and not rare, or says a PATCH may not ask even this — the trunk would then be recorded at the source instead (`generate` writes `ci.branch`, a MINOR and a slice of its own); a tiebreak would still not be added to the checker.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-25; AC-S22-13 and AC-S22-21 through it); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/tasks.md` (T020); `assets/toolkit/scripts/check-slice-scope.py` and `changelog.d/slice-scope-base.md` (at T020 and T023)
- **Status:** standing

## D34 — What do the other after-converge gaps findings on S22 become (G1–G3, G5–G11)?
- **Stage:** gaps (after converge) · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T09:36:00Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** The gaps pass (`drive-gaps`, read-only, at `2b43255`) ran the quickstart's steps as written (each gave its *Expect*) and found: G1 (HIGH) — the command the gate hands a developer, `git fetch origin <trunk>`, writes only `FETCH_HEAD` in a single-branch clone, so the gate repeats itself; the passed-over note has the same defect; G2 (MEDIUM) — a slice that commits `ci.branch: HEAD` is compared with `refs/remotes/origin/HEAD`, a symref a clone creates, which in a clone of a checkout sitting on the slice is the slice itself: a pass with the `project.json` edit unseen and no ref minted; G3 (MEDIUM) — a trunk named neither `main` nor `master` is held for the first time and the fragment's catch-up does not say so; G5 (LOW) — under a CI marker with a recorded name passed over, the output still carries *`git fetch origin main` would bring it*, which D32 says is never printed there; G6 (LOW) — a slice-shaped record is called *not a branch name*; G7 (LOW) — a non-string `ci.branch` is passed over without a word; G8 (LOW) — a no-base failure is printed under the header *a slice branch reaches outside what one slice may touch*, which is false there, and D31 says *one line*; G9 (LOW, older than the slice) — an unborn `HEAD` on `slice/S1` reads as a detached checkout with nothing to hold; G10 (LOW) — clauses of AC-S22-1, -2 and -19 with no assertion; G11 (LOW) — `CI=false` counts as a marker and nothing pins or says it. Questions: one command or two for a shallow single-branch clone (Q2); is first-time enforcement on a custom-named trunk still a PATCH (Q3).
- **Options:** (a) fix everything in the slice; (b) close in the slice what is a promise of S22 the code or its words do not keep and every unheld clause, state the reading where a standing decision already answers, place outside what is older than the slice — recommended by the stage (each finding's *to close*; D22 and D27 took S20's and S21's the same way); (c) carry all of it to the completion audit — not available for G1 and G2: the first makes the slice's own remedy false, the second is the door D30 closes, open by another name.
- **Decision:** (b). **Fixed in Phase 4, through failing tests:** G1 (T018) — every fetch the gate prints writes the ref it says is missing: `git fetch origin <trunk>:refs/remotes/origin/<trunk>`, on the no-trunk line and on the passed-over note; two steps stay two lines, each true where printed (Q2) — a shallow single-branch clone is told the fetch, then, if still without a common ancestor, `git fetch --unshallow origin`; a test runs the printed command and re-runs the gate. G2 (T019) — `HEAD`, in any case, is not a usable name from any source, and a candidate ref that is symbolic is not a trunk ref. G5–G8 (T021) — what the gate prints is true where it is printed: no fetch command in a forge's NOT-checked output; a slice-shaped record is said to be a slice branch, never the trunk; a `ci.branch` that is not a string is said to be passed over; a developer's no-base failure is one line of its own and not under the *reaches outside* header, lost records keeping theirs. T017, G10, G11 (T022, tests only) — both orders of the two refs of one name; the header naming `main` in AC-S22-1's three examples; the pass line naming `trunk` where `trunk` and `main` differ; a lost record in the unrelated-trunk state, attached and forge; `CI=false` counted, as D32's *non-empty* says. G3 with the fragment and docstring follow-ups of the above (T023, last). **Stated, not changed:** Q3 — PATCH stands: D30 named the custom-named trunk held *for the first time* when it claimed the level, the old answer there was the gate holding nothing, and the fragment now says so; G11's reading is D32's. **Placed outside the slice (Parking Lot):** G9, beside the `current_branch()` line.
- **Why:** owner brief, *Taste* — a gate says what it checked and what to do in one line a person can act on, and a command that does not do what the line says is worse than none; D30's local promise is what G2 breaks; constitution V's one capability keeps `current_branch()` out. None of the fixes changes what the merge root or CI checks.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person prefers one combined command for a shallow clone, or wants the unborn-`HEAD` case closed here.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-7 and AC-S22-14 amended; AC-S22-26 to AC-S22-28); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/tasks.md` (T018–T023); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing

## D35 — Where do the adversary's findings on S22 go (A1–A5, B1–B6), and the hand's notes?
- **Stage:** Phase 4 (adversary triage) · **Slice:** S22-slice-scope-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T10:12:00Z · **Iteration:** 5
- **Question:** The pass (two seams, at `bc919d6`) confirmed eleven findings, none `CRITICAL`. New with the slice: B1 (HIGH, a regression) — a recorded name whose ref shares no history ends the search, so a slice that commits `ci.branch: zz` and pushes an unrelated `zz` turns a refusal into *NOT checked* under a CI marker with full history; A1 — an honest target with no common history leaves the branch-chosen base standing; A2 — a NUL or lone surrogate in `ci.branch` is a traceback; B3, A5 — the printed fetch runs what a slice put in `ci.branch` when pasted, and an unusable value is echoed raw; A4 — the line names the recorded trunk beside the target's commit; B5 — the printed fetch cannot work without a remote named `origin`; B4 — where git cannot read the checkout, the new sentence about history is untrue. Older than the slice, made worse or reached by it: B2 (HIGH) — a `git diff` that fails reads as no changes, and the pass line now names a commit never compared with; A3 — an unbounded read through a committed symlink. Older and unchanged: B6 — a rebase in progress reads as a detached checkout. The hand's notes: (1) a recorded name with a locally minted ref still empties the local gate; (2) is A4; (3) the no-trunk line prints its fetch twice in an adopted repository; (4) refused paths still say *Land it on `main`* where the trunk is not `main`; (5) an empty `ci.branch` is passed over silently; (6) `refs/tags/main` is called *not a branch name*; (7) quickstart step 6 never commits its change, and `make` turns the gate's exit 1 into 2.
- **Options:** (a) fix everything in S22; (b) fix in S22, through failing tests, what the slice's own code or words broke or made untrue, with each fix closing its class; apply the standing answers of D31 and D32 wherever the gate *could not compare*; place outside the slice what is older and another capability — recommended by the stage (each finding's smallest fix; `delivery/commands/adversary.md`: only an open `CRITICAL` goes ahead of every slice; D22, D27 and D29 took earlier passes the same way); (c) a slice for the lot — not available for B1, a regression in this slice's diff.
- **Decision:** (b). **T024 (B1, A1) — a name that cannot be compared with is not a base and not the end of the search:** a trunk candidate with a ref and no common history is passed over for the next name (`main`, then `master`), and said so; only when no candidate yields a base is there none. A usable pull-request target with a ref and no common history is no base at all — the gate answers as D31 and D32 say for no base, naming the target — never the branch-chosen base. **T025 (A2, A3, B4) — the gate ends in a verdict whatever it is given:** `git()` returns nothing on any `ValueError`; every file the checker reads from the working tree is read only where it is a regular file under a size cap; where git cannot read the checkout at all (`git rev-parse --git-dir` fails) the gate keeps the exit it had before the slice, 0, and says on stderr that git could not read the checkout, with git's own first line, in place of any sentence about branches or history. **T026 (B2) — a comparison that could not run is *could not compare*:** where a base was found and `git diff` or `git ls-files` fails, a developer's checkout exits 1 with one line naming the base and git's own first line; a forge's checkout says *NOT checked* with that reason, exit 0 (D31 answers 2 and 3, D32); the pass line is never printed. **T027 (B3, A5, B5, A4, the hand's notes 2 and 3) — what the gate prints is safe to paste and true:** a `git fetch` is printed only where the name is made of `[A-Za-z0-9._/-]` and a remote named `origin` exists — otherwise the line says which branch is missing and prints no command; a recorded value that is not a usable name is never echoed raw (control characters dropped, cut to 80 characters); where the pull-request target's base won, the line names the target and says the pull request targets it; the no-base line does not repeat a fetch the passed-over note already gave. **T028 — the fragment and the docstring follow.** **Stated, not changed:** the hand's note 1 is D30's local promise as written and the fragment says so; note 5 is D34's reading (an absent, `null` or blank record stays silent); note 6 — a name under another `refs/` prefix is not a branch name, and the sentence is true. **Quickstart (host):** step 6 commits its change; a line says `make` reports the gate's exit 1 as 2. **Parking Lot:** B6 with the other `current_branch()` lines; whether a gate that cannot ask git should fail instead of exiting 0 (B4's exit — older than the slice, and in CI a person's, beside `S24`); refused paths saying *Land it on `main`* where the trunk has another name (note 4 — the script's older messages).
- **Why:** A developer trusts the gate's refusal and its green alike; B1 lets a slice switch the check off in exactly the project that followed the gate's own advice and fetched history, and A1 prints a pass for a branch compared with itself — both inside D30's promise (*nothing the branch commits or pushes moves the base forward*). A command the gate hands a person to paste is the gate's word (owner brief, *Taste*: one line a person can act on) and must not carry a slice's text into a shell. B2's and B4's fixes add no new judgement: *could not compare* already has its answers in D31 and D32, and where the exit was 0 before the slice in a state no slice can cause, it stays 0 until a person says CI may fail (owner brief, *Always ask a person*). Constitution V's one capability keeps the rebase reading and the messages' vocabulary out. Nothing changes what the merge root or CI checks. Not an ADR: each is a refactor of one function and its tests.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person wants a recorded trunk that shares no history with the branch to fail outright instead of falling to `main` — then T024's first half becomes a developer's exit 1 naming the record — or says a gate that cannot ask git must fail everywhere, which moves B4's exit into `S24`'s approval.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S22-29 to AC-S22-32); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/tasks.md` (T024–T028); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/adversary-log.md`; `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S22-slice-scope-base/quickstart.md`
- **Status:** standing

## D36 — In a project that sits in a subdirectory of its git repository, which uncommitted changes does the refusal look at, and how does it name them?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S23-refusal-in-subdirectory · **When:** 2026-10-03T10:57:22Z · **Iteration:** 6
- **Question:** The split's first example says the refresh *refuses naming the file*. `git status` spells a path from the repository's top (`sub/delivery/docs/convergence.md`) and a run spells what it writes from the project (`delivery/docs/convergence.md`). Which spelling is compared, recorded and printed, and what of a change outside the project — including one whose path from the top happens to spell a path the run writes?
- **Options:** (a) compare, record and print every path relative to the project, and look only at changes under the project's directory — recommended by the stage: it is what the top-of-repository case already does, and the split's third example says every answer there is today's; (b) print the path from the repository's top — the person is told a name no other line of slipwai's output uses, and `written.json` would change its spelling at the top or carry two; (c) refuse on any uncommitted change anywhere in the repository — the refusal this module was written to replace (its own docstring), and it would stop `/ground`'s one-commit sequence for a neighbour's work.
- **Decision:** (a). Only changes under the project's directory are looked at; each is spelled relative to the project when compared with what the run writes, when recorded in `.delivery-tools/written.json`, and when printed. A change elsewhere in the repository never refuses a run and is never recorded — nothing here writes there — and one at the top whose path spells a project path (today refused by mistake) is among them.
- **Why:** The developer reads one name for a file wherever their project sits, and the refusal protects exactly where a run writes — `src/slipwai/uncommitted.py`'s docstring: *a run can only lose that where it writes*; `story-split.md`, S23's examples one and three.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person wants the path printed from the repository's top so it can be pasted into a `git` command run there.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S23-1, AC-S23-5, AC-S23-7)
- **Status:** standing

## D37 — What does the first run after the fix do with regenerated files an earlier factory left uncommitted in a subdirectory project?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S23-refusal-in-subdirectory · **When:** 2026-10-03T10:57:22Z · **Iteration:** 6
- **Question:** Before the fix, `stamp()` recorded nothing where the project is in a subdirectory, so slipwai's own uncommitted regeneration there has no digest. Once the refusal works, the next `--confirm` or `--refresh` meets those files and cannot tell them from a person's edit. Refuse, or let them through?
- **Options:** (a) refuse, naming them; commit or stash once clears it, and the fragment says so — recommended by the stage: the slice's value is *never writes over uncommitted work*, and the owner brief's priority 5 puts a wrong pass below a slow one; (b) treat any unrecorded change as slipwai's own on the first run — writes over a person's edit in exactly the layout the slice exists for; (c) re-derive what slipwai would have written and compare — the module records by digest because the record moves between answers (`tests/test_uncommitted.py`, third test), so the comparison is wrong whenever a row moved.
- **Decision:** (a). No special case: an uncommitted change with no matching digest is refused. `changelog.d/`'s fragment says what a repository adopted in a subdirectory may meet once — a refusal naming files slipwai itself left — and that committing or stashing them clears it.
- **Why:** For the developer the cost is one commit they were about to make anyway (`/ground` ends in one); the other options cost work they cannot get back.
- **Decided by:** host (stage recommendation)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person says mid-`/ground` repositories in a subdirectory are common enough to want a one-time flag that takes the leftover as slipwai's own.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S23-9, AC-S23-10)
- **Status:** standing

## D40 — What does the refusal do where `GIT_DIR` is exported with no `GIT_WORK_TREE` and the adopted project sits in a subdirectory of its repository?
- **Stage:** convergence (pass 1, T012) · **Slice:** S23-refusal-in-subdirectory · **When:** 2026-10-03T11:29:55Z · **Iteration:** 6
- **Question:** With `GIT_DIR=<absolute>/.git` exported and the project in `sub/`, git takes `sub/` for the top of the work tree: `adopt --refresh` exits 2 on a clean tree naming 284 files, and *commit or stash* cannot clear it. With `GIT_DIR=.git` (relative) git finds no repository from `sub/`: exit 0, and a person's edit to `delivery/docs/convergence.md` is written over. Neither is answered by the criteria, the plan or D36–D38; neither is a regression (before S23 a subdirectory project was never refused); at the top both spellings answer correctly. What does `changed()` in `src/slipwai/uncommitted.py` do there?
- **Options:** (a) leave it — the person exported git's own override and `git status` in that shell says the same; one Parking Lot line — recommended by the stage; (b) run the two git calls with `GIT_DIR`, `GIT_WORK_TREE`, `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_PREFIX` removed from their environment; (c) refuse with one sentence naming the variable.
- **Decision:** (a). Nothing in `src/slipwai/uncommitted.py` changes in S23; T012 closes as decided with no files, no criterion is added, and the fragment and `VERSION` stand as they are. One line goes to the Parking Lot for the completion audit to place as a slice behind the PRD's slices (D39) or rule out. That line records that (b) is ruled out by evidence, and that if the state is taken up the shape is (c), narrowed: where a git call fails or the work tree is misplaced *and* `GIT_DIR` is set with no `GIT_WORK_TREE`, refuse in one sentence naming the variable; never strip the environment. Evidence, scratch repositories under `/tmp/d40`, git 2.53.0, each through `slipwai adopt --refresh` in `sub/`: `GIT_DIR` with `GIT_WORK_TREE` answers correctly today (edit refused, clean tree exit 0), including where the repository is kept outside the work tree; under (b) that last layout exits 0 and the edit is written over. `GIT_DIR` absolute alone refuses and loses nothing. `GIT_DIR=.git` relative alone is the one case that loses the edit, and in that shell `git status` itself fails with `fatal: not a git repository: '.git'`. Git 2.53 does not set a relative `GIT_DIR` for hooks, `!` aliases or `rebase --exec` run from `sub/`; in a linked worktree commit hooks get an absolute `GIT_DIR` alone, the case that refuses.
- **Why:** The developer who keeps a repository outside its work tree on purpose is refused correctly today and would lose an edit in silence under (b); the developer who loses one today is in a shell where git already says it cannot find the repository, which AC-S23-8 answers as written (*one git cannot read … nothing is refused, as today*). The split defers *any other change to what the refusal protects*, the finding is `LOW`, and the owner's D39 puts a defect slice opened from here on behind the PRD's slices unless it is `CRITICAL`. S23 keeps its one demo.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person or a tool is found to run slipwai with a relative `GIT_DIR` and no `GIT_WORK_TREE` from a project's subdirectory in ordinary use — a wrapper, a CI runner, or a git version that sets it for hooks — which would make the loss reachable and the narrowed (c) worth a slice now.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S23-refusal-in-subdirectory/tasks.md` (T012); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing

## D41 — With no `git` on `PATH`, is the machine AC-S23-8's *not in a git repository, or one git cannot read*, and does S23 change what `adopt --refresh` does there?
- **Stage:** convergence (pass 1, T013) · **Slice:** S23-refusal-in-subdirectory · **When:** 2026-10-03T11:29:18Z · **Iteration:** 6
- **Question:** With no `git` on `PATH`, `slipwai adopt --refresh` ends on a `FileNotFoundError` traceback raised in `changed()` (`src/slipwai/uncommitted.py:39`, from `resurvey.py:167`), exit 1, nothing written — older than the slice and the same at the top of a repository. AC-S23-8 says no run ends on a traceback where the project is not in a git repository, or in one git cannot read. Is a machine without git that state, and does this slice change what happens there?
- **Options:** (a) treat it as *not a repository* — `None`, nothing refused, nothing recorded, the run goes on; (b) a one-line refusal that the command needs git; (c) leave it, out of this feature, as a Parking Lot line — recommended by the stage.
- **Decision:** (c), taking the stage's recommendation, with AC-S23-8 narrowed so the slice does not claim what it does not hold. A machine with no `git` is not AC-S23-8's state: both of its states are answers git gives, and here git never answered. No production code changes in S23 for this, and T013 closes as decided with no test. AC-S23-8 becomes: *"Given a machine where `git` runs, and a project directory that is not in a git repository, or in one git cannot read, then nothing is refused and nothing is recorded, as today, and no run ends on a traceback. A machine with no `git` on `PATH` is neither state and is not changed by this slice (D41): the run ends as it did before, writing nothing."* T010's sweep stands as written, reading *either of the two git calls failing* as git answering non-zero. The Parking Lot line records that the answer, when placed, is (b) and never (a): one line saying the command needs git, exit 2, nothing written, swept over every `subprocess` call to `git` that an adopted project's commands reach. That sweep includes `refuse_uncommitted()` in `src/slipwai/add_service.py:53–58`, whose comment says *or no git* but which raises the same `FileNotFoundError`. Under D39 it goes behind the PRD's slices, because the finding is LOW.
- **Why:** Today the developer without git loses nothing: an ugly exit 1 and no file touched. Option (a) is the one that could cost them work. With `changed()` answering `None`, the refresh is no longer stopped at the refusal on a machine that cannot see a person's uncommitted edit; a container with the checkout mounted and no git is the likely case. From there it either meets another unguarded `git` call (`structure.py:251` and `:262`, `quick_wins.py:80`) and ends on a traceback anyway, or, once those are swept too, writes over the edit in silence. That is the loss the slice exists to stop, and D37 already put a wrong pass below an inconvenient stop. Option (b) is the message the owner brief's *Taste* asks for, but it is new behaviour at the top of a repository as well. The split's *Defers* column (*any other change to what the refusal protects*), D38's *one slice, one demo*, and D39's order all put it outside S23.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person reports adopted repositories being driven on machines without git (an agent sandbox, a CI image) often enough that the traceback is what they meet — then (b) is taken as a slice ahead of its place in the order; never (a).
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S23-refusal-in-subdirectory/tasks.md` (T013); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S23-8, narrowed); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing

## D42 — What do converge pass 1's other findings on S23 become (T010, T011, T014, T015)?
- **Stage:** convergence (pass 1) · **Slice:** S23-refusal-in-subdirectory · **When:** 2026-10-03T11:31:09Z · **Iteration:** 6
- **Question:** The pass converged with four findings that need no product decision: T010 (`MEDIUM`) — AC-S23-8's *one git cannot read* has no example; T011 (`LOW`) — nothing observes the rename rule, and an entry whose *second* status letter is `R` or `C` has its origin read as a change of its own, which in a subdirectory project can now refuse a run naming a file nobody changed; T014 (`LOW`) — the placements do not repeat AC-S23-3's second run or AC-S23-5's file outside the project; T015 (`LOW`) — a refused `--decline` is told what `slipwai adopt --confirm` writes. Code, a stated reading, or the Parking Lot?
- **Options:** (a) all four to the Parking Lot — the slice would go to its demo with a criterion half held and a wrong refusal it made reachable; (b) all four in this slice's Phase 4, each through its test, as the tasks' sweeps say — recommended by the stage and the way D22, D27 and D34 placed a pass's small findings on the slice's own surface; (c) T015 to the Parking Lot as older than the slice.
- **Decision:** (b). T010 and T014 are tests only. T011 changes `changed()` so an entry carrying a second path is one entry whichever column says so, swept over `R` and `C` in either column, at the top and in a subdirectory. T015 changes the verb `confirm` hands `refuse_foreign()` so the message names the flags the run was given; it is the message a person now meets in a subdirectory project for the first time, at the seam this slice changes, and costs one line. The fragment gains a clause for each of T011 and T015 where a person could have met the difference; still a PATCH.
- **Why:** Each is on the refusal this slice is about and small enough to ride in it; none is a new capability. D39 sends *new defect slices* behind the PRD's slices — these are tasks of the slice in flight, not slices.

## D45 — Of FR-004's five names, which are pruned wherever a directory carries the name, and is `target` pruned only where it is build output?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T13:18:19Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** FR-004 says `check-imports` and `check-migrations` MUST prune `.venv`, `node_modules`, `target/`, `__pycache__` and `.git` before descending. SC-007 says the full gate's findings on the same tree are identical before and after every story. A source directory can legitimately be called `target` (a Java package, or a bounded context under a service's `src/`), and pruning it by name would silently stop the gate reading code it reads today. Which names are pruned at any depth, is any pruned only where it is recognisably a build or tool directory, and is the list closed at five?
- **Options:** (a) prune all five by name at any depth, as FR-004 reads literally; (b) prune `.venv`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__` and `.git` by name at any depth, and `target` only where it is recognisably build output; (c) something else. The stage recommended none; the host leaned towards a closed list.
- **Decision:** (b), with the list closed at the five names for this slice. **Four names by name alone:** a directory called `.venv`, `node_modules`, `__pycache__` or `.git` is not descended into, at any depth, in every walk the two scripts make — `source_files`, rule 4's walk of each web app and rule 5's walk of each context-holding service in `check-imports.py`, and `migrations` in `check-migrations.py`. **`target` by one test:** a directory called `target` is pruned only where the directory that holds it also holds a `pom.xml` file — Maven's output directory, and Maven is the only build tool the factory generates that writes one; any other `target` is descended into exactly as today. **Not taken as the test:** asking git whether the directory is ignored (the answer would depend on git being present and on a file the project edits), and *not under `src/`* (a guess about layout an adopted repository need not share). **How SC-007 reads for this slice:** no finding on a file outside a pruned directory changes; a finding that existed only on a file inside a pruned directory goes away, and the fragment says so. **Parking Lot:** other tools' `target/` (Cargo, sbt) in adopted repositories, and other output names (`dist`, `build`, `.build`, `coverage`, `.pytest_cache`, `.ruff_cache`, `.mypy_cache`, `.terraform`), wait for a measurement that shows a walk needs them; `dist` and `build` carry the same source-name ambiguity as `target` and would each need their own test.
- **Why:** The developer trusts the gate to read all of their code, and the owner brief names the one thing that makes this work pointless: a faster loop that lets through what today's gate catches. `target` is an ordinary domain word — a sales target, a delivery target — and the generated `.gitignore` for Python, TypeScript and Go projects has no `target/` line, so a context at `apps/<service>/src/target/` is tracked source there and every rule reads it today; pruning it by name would remove a check on real code in CI and at the merge root, which constitution I forbids (*no check is removed anywhere to make the loop faster*) and the brief lists under *Always ask a person*, so (a) is not available as written. `pom.xml` costs one stat, is deterministic (priority 5), and is exact for what the factory generates. The other four are safe by name: two are dot-directories, one is a Python dunder, and `node_modules` has its meaning from Node's own module resolution; the generated `.gitignore` already ignores them at every depth. What vanishes with them is findings on code that is not the project's — a dependency's `domain/` package under a service's `.venv`, an installed package's `migrations/0001_*.py`, Maven's copy of `db/migration/V*.sql` under `target/classes`. Closed at five because the spec names five and each further name is its own judgement about whether it can be source (priority 2). Nothing was run for this entry; it comes from reading the two scripts, the factory's gitignore writer and the scratch skeleton.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a generated or adopted project is shown where Maven's output directory is a `target` with no `pom.xml` beside it, or where a `pom.xml` sits in a directory that also holds a source directory called `target` — the test would then become the build directory the service's own record names, and a person would confirm it, because it moves what CI reads. (The skipper's confidence: high on the four names and on not pruning `target` by name; medium on `pom.xml` as the whole test.)
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-3 to AC-S01-5); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S01's row, Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing

## D46 — On which checkouts does `check-codegraph` hash only changed files and skip the integrity check, what is *since the last sync* measured from, and what does it do when it cannot tell?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T13:19:25Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** FR-022 (the part in S01) says `check-codegraph` MUST hash only files git reports changed since the last sync and run the SQLite integrity check in CI only; the split's third example says a branch run hashes the one changed file and CI also runs the integrity check. Unstated: (1) on which checkouts the narrowed run applies, and what a local run on the trunk does; (2) what *since the last sync* is measured from, given nothing records when the gate last found the index current, so that the gate can never report *current* for a file whose content differs from what the index read; (3) what it does with no usable memory, git unable to answer, or a detached `HEAD`; and what the pass line reports.
- **Options:** *Where:* (a) every local run, the trunk included — FR-022's letter; (b) every local run off the trunk; (c) only a `slice/<id>` branch in a developer's checkout, everything else as today. *Measured from:* (d) the index's own `files.indexed_at` against file mtimes, no memory; (e) a remembered commit and `git diff` against it; (f) a memory the gate writes only after a passing comparison, bound to the tree content and the index content it vouched for and to the scripts that wrote it. *Cannot tell:* (g) fail; (h) pass narrowed anyway; (i) run today's whole check and say why. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (c), (f) and (i). **Where:** the narrowed run happens only where the branch name git reports has the shape `slice/<id>` (the test `check-slice-scope` uses) and no CI marker is set (`CI`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS` or `GITLAB_CI` non-empty, D32's list). Every other run does exactly what the script does today — every tracked file hashed, the integrity check run, the same repair, the same exit codes: the trunk, any branch not shaped `slice/<id>` (this run's `adopt-method`, D12), a detached `HEAD`, and any run with a CI marker. **Unavailable:** (a), and any reading that narrows a local run on the trunk — the constitution's MUST (the merge root runs the full gate, no check removed anywhere) forbids it and the owner brief's *Always ask a person* covers it. **How FR-022 is read:** *in CI only* means *where the full gate runs: CI and the merge root*, the shape FR-023 writes out as *always on `main` and in CI*; read to the letter the integrity check would run nowhere, because the index is not in version control and a CI checkout normally has none. For the `slice/<id>` case FR-022, SC-009 and the split's example are already the owner's answer, so no person is needed, and nothing CI does changes. **What the memory must satisfy** (the design is the plan's): a narrowed run never reports *current* while a tracked file the index holds a row for has bytes whose SHA-256 differs from that row, or a file that became tracked or changed content since the memory was written has no row. For that the memory must (1) be written only by a run that compared and found the index current, never after a failure or a skip; (2) cover uncommitted content — a file dirty when it was written is hashed on every narrowed run, or its content is remembered, so a later revert is seen; (3) count as changed everything git reports between the vouched-for tree and now: committed, staged, unstaged, newly tracked, renamed; (4) always hash a file git has been told not to report (`assume-unchanged`, `skip-worktree`), or run whole where any exists; (5) read the index's rows fresh on every run and re-hash any file whose row differs from, is new since, or is gone since what was vouched for — a different or rebuilt database means the whole run; (6) not rest on `files.indexed_at` alone, and never on a file's mtime to skip one; (7) carry in its key the content hashes of `check-codegraph.py` and `agents/code_index.py`, and be unusable where either differs; (8) be neither read nor written where a CI marker is set; (9) leave nothing `git status` reports, and go when the index it describes goes; (10) stay correct where `.codegraph/` was copied from another checkout. A `touch` with no change of bytes is not a change. **Repair:** a narrowed run that finds drift syncs as today, compares again under (5), and renews the memory only on a pass. **Cannot tell:** the run does today's whole check and says why in one clause; it never fails for that reason alone and never passes narrower — no memory, a key mismatch, a memory git cannot compare with the present tree (its commit gone after a rebase and prune; a commit that is merely unreachable still compares), git unable to list changes, an unreadable memory, and any SQLite error reading the index on a narrowed run (with the integrity check skipped, a corrupt database must not come out as *schema may have moved on — skipped*). Where git cannot answer at all, today's *not a checkout — skipped* stands. **Pass line:** a narrowed pass says in one line how many files it hashed out of how many the index holds, that it compared only what changed since its last whole comparison and when that was, and that the integrity check was not run here and runs in the full gate; an unchanged tree reports 0 hashed. A whole run on the trunk or in CI prints today's line byte for byte; a whole run on a slice branch that could not narrow prints today's line plus the one clause; a failure prints today's report. **The split's exclusion** — *anything about stamps* — is read as the verify stamp of S03 and FR-023: this memory is the check's own, and without it FR-022's own words cannot be met.
- **Why:** The developer sees how long a slice takes and whether the gate can still be believed. On a slice branch this check runs about three times per slice over every tracked file; hashing the one file that moved is the saving the owner asked for, and the full comparison still happens before the change reaches `main`. A narrowed green that hides a stale index is the specific harm — a stale index answers *nothing calls that* and the agent builds on it — so the memory vouches for content on both sides, the tree's and the index's, never for a commit name or a timestamp (owner priority 5). The host's four leads were checked and each holds: a dirty-then-reverted file is absent from a diff against the remembered commit; `indexed_at` is CodeGraph's column and its discipline is unverified; a rebase can leave the commit unresolvable; `main()` exits at *no .codegraph/* before any check. Limiting the narrowing to `slice/<id>` costs speed on a branch like `adopt-method`, where slices land directly; that is the safe side — a merge root is not always named `main` (D12), and the merge tree will add more such nodes. D30 to D33 are untouched; their slice-branch and CI tests are reused, not redefined. Not verified: CodeGraph's schema beyond the three columns the script reads, since this tree has no index to open; no reproduction was run.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person says a local trunk run, or every off-trunk branch, should be narrowed too — that widens the *Where* rule, and for the trunk it is their call alone, since it changes what the merge root checks. The memory's rules would stand either way.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-10 to AC-S01-21); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S01's row)
- **Status:** standing

## D47 — What does *`check-imports` enumerates at most 100 entries* count, on which tree, and where is the count said?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T13:18:00Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** SC-005 and S01's first example say `check-imports` enumerates at most 100 entries on the generated skeleton and reports the count. Unstated: (1) what an entry is, and whether it is counted once per run or once per walk; (2) which skeleton, in what state; (3) where the count is reported, and whether also on failure; (4) whether `check-migrations` reports one too; (5) whether exceeding 100 on a real project is a failure, a warning or nothing.
- **Options:** (1) files only; distinct paths; or every directory entry the script lists, summed over the run. (2) freshly generated; or after `./init` and installs. (3) on the existing pass line; a second line; stderr; with or without the failure path. (4) `check-imports` only; or both scripts. (5) a failure; a warning; or nothing — the stage's lean, a measurement held by a factory test and never a limit on a project.
- **Decision:** **(1)** An entry is one name a directory listing returns to the script — file, directory or anything else — and the count is the sum over the whole run: a directory read twice is counted twice. A pruned directory (FR-004's five as D45 reads them, and no others) is the one entry it is, and nothing inside it is listed. **(2)** The 100 is measured on the reference fixture as freshly generated, nothing installed: Python backend, `event-modelling` profile, `react-vite` frontend, target `none`. There the count must be at most 100 and equal to an independent enumeration of `apps/` and `packages/` with the pruned names left out (79 today). The installed state is held by a second property, not a number: planting a populated `.venv`, `node_modules` and `__pycache__` raises the count by exactly the number of directories planted, whatever they hold. **(3)** The count goes on the existing pass line, on stdout, with today's words unbroken before it: `check-imports: inward dependency rule holds (79 directory entries read)`. No second line. On failure the output is byte for byte what it was — the findings on stderr, exit 1, no count. **(4)** `check-migrations` reports the same way, a parenthetical closing its existing pass line; it carries no numeric bound, but the same planted-directories property and the same single listing. **(5)** Over 100 on a real project: nothing — no failure, no warning, no setting.
- **Why:** A developer waits on this gate three times a slice. Today the script lists the same tree three times for the layer rules and each browser app again — 256 listings on a skeleton of 79 entries, before any `.venv` exists (measured on the scratch skeleton by the skipper). Counting distinct paths would pass that unchanged; counting every listing makes *at most 100* mean each directory is read once, which is the owner brief's priority 2. A fixed number on an installed tree would depend on what an installer wrote; the planted-directories property states the actual promise — what is inside a pruned directory costs nothing (priority 5). The pass line is where the owner's *Taste* puts it: a gate says what it checked in one line. The only verbatim reader of either line in the factory is one `assertIn` on stdout in the ratchet's tests, which a suffix keeps green. Failure output staying as it is gives SC-007 a measurement for these two scripts, and a count beside findings tells the person fixing them nothing. Both scripts report because FR-004 names both and *never descends into either* has no observation without a count. No limit on a project: that would be a new check failing on a project's size, which nobody asked for.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person or tool is found to compare either pass line whole rather than by its opening words — the count then moves to a second stdout line and the first line stays byte-identical.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-1, AC-S01-2, AC-S01-6, AC-S01-7); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S01's row)
- **Status:** standing

## D48 — On a slice branch with a usable memory, what does `check-codegraph` answer for a sound database whose `files` table it cannot read?
- **Stage:** convergence · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T13:46:00Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** Converge pass 1 (T019): AC-S01-18 says a narrowed run that cannot read the index's rows is *never a pass and never skipped* and also that it *goes whole and answers as AC-S01-12*; for a database that passes SQLite's integrity check but carries no readable `files` table the whole run's answer today is `… schema may have moved on — skipped`, exit 0. Which reading stands?
- **Options:** (a) the whole run's answer is the answer: the narrowed attempt goes whole, the integrity check runs, and a sound database with another schema is skipped in today's words — *never skipped* is about damage (recommended: it is D46's own text); (b) refuse the skip on a slice branch — a new failure the trunk and CI do not have.
- **Decision:** (a). A narrowed attempt that cannot read the rows goes whole, with the integrity check: a damaged database fails or is rebuilt as today, and a sound one whose schema moved is skipped in today's words, with no clause added to that line. AC-S01-18 is reworded to say so; a hold test pins the skip on a slice branch; no production change.
- **Why:** D46 says the run *does today's whole check* where it cannot tell, and gives the reason for the SQLite rule as *a corrupt database must not come out as schema may have moved on — skipped* — with the integrity check skipped. Once the run is whole that check has run, and what is left is CodeGraph's schema having moved, which the script has always treated as CodeGraph's business rather than a reason to fail a build. (b) would make a slice branch stricter than the merge root, which nobody asked for.
- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D46)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person wants an unreadable schema to fail the gate — then it fails everywhere, the trunk and CI included, and that is theirs to say because it changes what the merge root checks.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-18); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S01-gate-walks/tasks.md` (T019)
- **Status:** standing

## D49 — How does a narrowed `check-codegraph` run know a file it does not hash still has the bytes it had, when git's comparison normalises some changes away, and does D46's rule 6 stand as written?
- **Stage:** convergence · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T13:50:00Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** D46 lets `check-codegraph` on a `slice/<id>` branch hash only what changed since its last whole comparison, under a must-never: a narrowed run never reports *current* while a tracked file's bytes differ from the index's row. Rule 3 takes *everything git reports* as changed and rule 6 says the memory never rests on a file's mtime to skip one. Converge pass 1 (T016) found git's word is not about raw bytes: under the generated `.gitattributes` (`* text=auto eol=lf`) a file rewritten with CRLF is absent from `git diff --name-only <commit>`, so the narrowed run printed `hashed 0` and exited 0 while the whole run failed naming the file. The class is every working-tree change git's comparison normalises away (line endings, `core.autocrlf`, clean filters, `ident`), and after `git add` not even `git status` shows it. The index's `content_hash` is of raw bytes. How does a narrowed run know an unhashed file is unchanged, and does rule 6 stand?
- **Options:** (a) the memory also records each indexed tracked file's size and mtime in nanoseconds, a file whose either differs is hashed, a file modified at or after the memory's writing is always hashed, and rule 6 is amended to *never on mtime alone*; (b) narrow only where git's comparison is byte-exact, which is nowhere in a generated project; (c) give up narrowing the hashing, so FR-022's first half and SC-009's *hashes one file* go back to a person; (d) something else sound. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (a), strengthened to the evidence git itself uses, and it overrides rule 6 of D46 only; every other rule of D46 stands and D46 stays `standing`. **The record:** when the gate writes its memory it records, for every tracked file the index holds a row for, the file's size, modification time and change time in nanoseconds, and file identity (inode), as many of the four as the platform reports, read from the file as it was opened for hashing, before its bytes were read. **The candidates:** a narrowed run stats every such file and hashes, in addition to everything rules 2 to 5 already make a candidate, any file whose record is missing, cannot be read, or differs in any recorded field. **Written during the run:** a file whose modification or change time is not safely older than the start of the run that vouched for it is hashed on every narrowed run until a later passing run vouches for it again; *safely* means by the filesystem's own timestamp granularity, and where the gate cannot know that, two seconds. **Renewal:** a narrowed pass renews the record of each file it hashed and found equal, so a `touch`, a checkout or a rebase costs one hash once, not for ever, and is still not a change. **Cannot tell:** a memory without these records, or with one the run cannot use, means the whole run with D46's one clause. **Rule 6 as amended:** the memory never rests on `files.indexed_at` alone and never skips a file on its modification time alone; it may skip a file only where git reports no change *and* the file's size, modification time, change time and identity are all what the gate recorded when it last hashed that file and found it equal to its row. **The must-never as restated:** a narrowed run never reports *current* for a file whose bytes differ from its row unless every one of those four reads exactly as it did at that hash. **The residual, in words the fragment can carry:** *a file whose bytes were changed while its size, modification time, change time and identity all read as before — a clock set back, a filesystem that keeps no such times, or, on a platform with no change time, a tool that restores the modification time on a same-size rewrite — is not seen by a narrowed run; the next whole run sees it: the trunk, any branch not named `slice/<id>`, or any run after the record is deleted.* It does not need a person, and goes into the cruise report as an accepted residual. **Unavailable:** nothing; (b) and (c) are available and rejected. One more example is owed beside T016's: a same-size rewrite in place with the modification time restored, which the narrowed run must fail on as the whole run does.
- **Why:** The developer waits on this check about three times a slice, and the saving the owner asked for in FR-022 is hashing the one file that moved. (b) delivers that nowhere, because `* text=auto eol=lf` covers every file in every generated project. (c) hands the requirement back although the owner's own words already answer it: FR-022 says *files git reports changed*, and git's report is itself a stat comparison. Checked by the skipper in a scratch repository (git 2.53.0): with `core.trustctime=false` and `core.checkStat=minimal`, a same-size rewrite in place with its mtime restored is absent from both `git status` and `git diff <commit>`; under default configuration git sees it only because the change time moved. So rule 6 as written was never satisfiable beside rule 3: D46 already rested on stat data through git, without naming it. Recording the same facts directly closes T016's class, because the gate's record is of the raw file, not of git's normalised view. In the same repository the CRLF rewrite moved the file's mtime and ctime; before `git add`, `status`, `diff-files` and `ls-files -m` name it and `git diff --name-only <commit>` does not; after `git add` nothing names it, the worktree and blob SHA-256 differ, and git's index has taken the new stat — which is why git's index cannot be the witness and the gate keeps its own. Change time and identity are included because size and mtime alone leave an ordinary residual (`cp -p`, `rsync -t`, an archive extraction); with them the residual needs a clock set back. Owner priority 5 is met as far as any gate standing on git can meet it: no false green is cached on evidence weaker than git's own, the key still carries the script hashes, and the memory is never read in CI. Constitution I holds: nothing is removed from the merge root or CI. The catch for the residual is the next local whole run, not CI, since CI normally has no index; the harm is a stale row in a developer's unversioned local index, never a change reaching `main`. Not verified: Windows (where Python's change time is the creation time) and network filesystems; both are covered only by *as many as the platform reports* and the granularity clause.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a narrowed run is shown reporting *current* for changed bytes on an ordinary developer filesystem with all four recorded facts unchanged and no clock moved — then narrowing the hashing is unsound here and (c) goes to a person as an unmet FR-022.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-18, AC-S01-21, AC-S01-23 to AC-S01-25); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S01-gate-walks/tasks.md` (T016); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S01-gate-walks/data-model.md`; `changelog.d/gate-walks.md`
- **Status:** standing
