## D52 — What makes a directory called `target` build output, such that a slice cannot make its own source one (adversary A1), and what does the gate do with a deployable recorded at a pruned directory (A4)?
- **Stage:** adversary · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T15:24:01Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** D45 decided that `check-imports` and `check-migrations` do not descend a directory called `target` where a `pom.xml` file sits beside it. The adversary showed (A1, `HIGH`, new with the slice) that a slice can commit that test: on a `slice/<id>` branch of a generated Python project, an empty `apps/service/src/shop/pom.xml` beside a context directory `target/` holding a `domain/` file that imports an adapter and a migration that drops a column makes both gates pass, at every depth and in every language, and `check-slice-scope` passes the branch because a file inside the slice's own service is the slice's to write. Before the slice the only in-tree switches on these gates were `project.json` and `scripts/`, both refused on a slice branch. Related (A4, `LOW`): a deployable recorded at a pruned directory is read by rules 4 and 5 and pruned by rules 1–3. What makes a `target` directory build output, such that a slice cannot make its own source one?
- **Options:** (a) the record decides: `target` is not descended only at `<path>/target` where `<path>` is a deployable `project.json` records with `language` `java` and `<path>/pom.xml` is a file, every other `target` read as before the slice; (b) keep D45's test and make `check-slice-scope` refuse a new `pom.xml` outside a Java deployable's root; (c) never prune `target`, and return that half of FR-004 to a person; (d) ask git whether the directory is ignored; (e) something else. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (a), with A4 closed by the same rule: the record outranks the name. **`target`:** a directory called `target` is not descended only where all three hold: its parent is the `path` of a deployable in `project.json` (compared as `x`, `x/` and `./x` alike); that record's `language` is the string `java`, whatever its `kind` and whether or not it is `generated`; and `<path>/pom.xml` is a file. **Every other `target` is read as before the slice:** a nested Maven module's below a Java deployable's root, one in a non-Java deployable whatever sits beside it, one under `packages/` with no record, and every one in a tree with no `project.json`, or one whose record is unreadable or carries a `path` or `language` that is not a string. **`check-migrations` reads `project.json` once** for this and nothing else; a missing or unreadable record means no `target` is pruned and its answer and exit are what they were before the slice, never a traceback it did not have. **A4:** a directory that is a recorded deployable's `path`, or lies on the way to one, is descended whatever it is called, in every walk of both scripts, so rules 1–3 and `check-migrations` read what rules 4 and 5 read. **Overrides one clause of D45:** its *Would reverse if* said a person would confirm the move to the record; this entry takes that move without parking, for the reason under *Why*; the rest of D45 (the four names, the closed list, the Parking Lot) stands. **Reads less than before the slice, outside what D45 allowed:** nothing; the pruned set is a strict subset of D45's, and A4's rule only adds reading. **Needs a person:** no. **Residual, stated and not coded:** in a recorded Java deployable a slice could force-add source under `<path>/target/` past the generated `target/` ignore line; that directory is Maven's to delete on `mvn clean`, no innocent layout puts source there, and it goes to the Parking Lot for the completion audit. **Not taken:** (b) changes a third gate and leaves the innocent case silent; (c) gives up a pruning the record makes safe; (d) `.gitignore` is the repository's own on a slice branch (AC-S20-16, D20), so it is exactly the slice-writable switch A1 found. **The fragment, in one sentence:** *`check-imports` and `check-migrations` skip a `target` directory only at the root of a deployable `project.json` records as Java with a `pom.xml` there — any other directory called `target` is read as before, and a deployable recorded inside a skipped name is still read.*
- **Why:** The developer's whole reason to trust a slice branch is that the slice cannot switch its own gate off. D45's test put that switch in a file the slice owns: one empty `pom.xml` and a context's domain code and migrations stop being read, on the branch, at the merge root and in CI, which is the one outcome the owner brief says makes this work pointless. `project.json` is the one in-tree fact about what a deployable is that a slice branch cannot write (the host files of `check-slice-scope.py`), and it was already the only switch on these gates before the slice, so the record adds no new trust. Checked by the skipper: a generated `java-quarkus` project records `"path": "apps/service"`, `"language": "java"`, with `pom.xml` at `apps/service/pom.xml` and `target/` in its `.gitignore`; `slipwai adopt` records `java` for a Maven candidate. The generated Java backends keep the full saving, and the cost falls only on nested Maven modules in adopted repositories, which are slower and never narrower (priority 5 over priority 2). No person is needed because the move is towards the gate as it was before the slice: nothing D45 reads stops being read, and D45's narrower gate has not shipped. A4 gets the same answer because one rule a developer can say in a sentence is better than two walks of one gate disagreeing, and it too can only add reading.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a slice branch is shown able to change what `project.json` records for a deployable and still pass `check-slice-scope`, or a real project is shown keeping tracked source under a recorded Java deployable's `target/` — then `target` is not pruned at all, and that half of FR-004 goes to a person (option c). (The skipper's confidence: high on the record as the test and on reading no less than before the slice; medium on leaving the Java force-add residual as a stated reading.)
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S01-4, AC-S01-5, AC-S01-8, AC-S01-22, AC-S01-26); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S01-gate-walks/tasks.md` (T030); `changelog.d/gate-walks.md`
- **Status:** standing

- **Stage:** adversary · **Slice:** S01-gate-walks · **When:** 2026-10-03T15:40:00Z · **Iteration:** 7
- **Question:** Beside A1 and A4 (D52), the pass found, all new with the slice: A2 `LOW` (`children()` in `check-migrations` leaves out a pruned directory, so a `contract:` marker naming one changes the verdict or the words on the project's own file), A3 `LOW` (an unreadable `apps/` now passes `check-migrations` with `0 directory entries read` where it ended on a traceback), F1 `MEDIUM` (a narrowed `check-codegraph` run reads the index's rows twice; a row that changes between the reads is never hashed and is recorded as vouched for, so later narrowed runs pass on it), F2 `MEDIUM` (the record is written through a fixed temporary name: a symbolic link committed at `.codegraph/gate-memory.json.tmp` has its target overwritten by the first passing run, on the trunk too, and `git status` then shows a deletion), F3 `LOW` (a FIFO at that name hangs the run). ## D54 — S24's fetch also gives `check-migrations` and `check-flags` a base in CI: is that inside D39's approval, and where is history fetched?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S24-ci-fetches-slice-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:17:34Z · **Iteration:** 8
- **Question:** S24 makes the generated `verify` job and the adopted delivery workflow fetch the history `check-slice-scope` needs (`fetch-depth: 0`; on GitLab `GIT_DEPTH: "0"`), approved in D39. `check-migrations` and `check-flags` run in the same job and choose a base from the same refs, so with history fetched their CI answers change on every pull request, slice branch or not. Is that consequence inside what the owner approved, what does S24 do about it, and where is history fetched?
- **Options:** (a) fetch full history on every run of the `verify` job and accept that the two gates hold in CI as they do on a developer's machine; (b) fetch only on pull requests from `slice/<id>` branches; (c) fetch everywhere and have the two gates read a CI marker to ignore a base they have; (d) `unavailable: a person's approval`, the slice blocked and the run taking the next ready slice; (e) something else. The stage recommended none.
- **Decision:** (d) — **`unavailable: a person's approval`** that CI's `check-migrations` and `check-flags` start holding their *new in this change* rules on pull requests. S24 is marked blocked for a person and the run takes the next ready slice (`S02-runner-bookkeeping`, then `S11-render-once`, D39's order). Nothing in S24 is built meanwhile, because the fetch is the slice and cannot be separated from the consequence. **What changes, exactly:** both gates only gain refusals; neither newly allows anything. `check-migrations` newly refuses a contracting migration whose `contract:` names an expand added in the same pull request; `check-flags` rule 3 newly refuses a flag declared in the pull request and seeded anything but `off`; push runs on the trunk are unchanged, since the base there is `HEAD`. **Which options need a person:** (a) needs one — it changes CI's answers on pull requests that are not slice branches, and S24's own third example, as it stood when D39 was given, says *every answer is today's* there. (b) needs one too — it keeps the third example true, but the two gates still tighten on slice pull requests, which neither the row nor D31 nor D39 says. (c) needs no person and is not available: a project whose maintainer already fetches history has these rules holding in CI today, and so does a developer's shell with `CI` set on a full clone; a marker would switch them off there, which removes a check (constitution I; owner brief, *Always ask a person*, first item), makes each gate's docstring false and has a gate ignore evidence it holds. (e) giving `check-slice-scope` a trunk ref under a name the other two do not read is (c) by another route; rejected. **The question for the person, ready to send:** *S24's `fetch-depth: 0` also gives `check-migrations` and `check-flags` a base in CI. On every pull request they would start refusing (1) a contracting migration whose expand is new in the same pull request and (2) a new flag seeded other than `off` — both already refused on a developer's machine; nothing is newly allowed; pushes to the trunk are unchanged. Approve fetching on every run of the `verify` job (recommended), or only on `slice/<id>` pull requests?* **If the person says yes to the recommendation, this entry becomes (a) with no second skipper round:** generated project — `fetch-depth: 0`, unconditional, on the `verify` job's `actions/checkout@v6` in `workflow()`, both events, no expression; `CONTAINER_CHECKOUT` and the integration jobs stay depth 1. Adopted, GitHub — the same on the `verify` job in `delivery_workflow()`; the smoke job untouched. Adopted, GitLab — `variables: GIT_DEPTH: "0"` on `verify-delivery` only in `gitlab_job()`; `smoke-delivery` untouched and no `rules:` added. S24's third example is rewritten by the host to: *a pull request from a branch that is not `slice/<id>`: `check-slice-scope`'s answer is today's; `check-migrations` and `check-flags` answer as they do on a developer's full clone*. The fragment (PATCH) says CI's `verify` job now fetches full history, that a slice pull request is held to its scope, that *NOT checked* in CI is now a failure, and that the two other gates' *new in this change* rules now hold in CI, naming both refusals; its catch-up paragraph says a workflow the project took over is not rewritten by `migrate`, so add `fetch-depth: 0` (GitLab: `GIT_DEPTH: "0"`) by hand, that an open pull request carrying either pattern goes red and is fixed by landing the expand first or seeding `off`, and that a repository with long history pays the full fetch on that one job. Still PATCH by `AGENTS.md`'s table — the same answers, generated better; no file, option, flag or setting is added. The mintable base in the two gates (Parking Lot, D31) stays there.
- **Why:** For the developer, option (a) is almost certainly right: a pull request that their own `make verify` refuses should not go green in CI, and owner priority 1 asks for the full gate in CI. But that is the skipper's reading of what the owner would want, and D31 already ruled that such a reading *is not that approval*; D32 held the same line. D39's words approve *S24's workflow change (D31's person's approval: yes)*; D31 describes that change only by its effect on `check-slice-scope`, says the other two gates' shallow stance *stands*, and S24's row promises non-slice pull requests are unaffected — an approval given on a description that says the opposite of the consequence does not cover it. The cost of asking is one message; the owner answered the last one the same day, and the run does not stall. (b) is not built meanwhile: it is also outside the approval; it makes two gates answer differently by branch name; on GitHub it needs an expression whose obvious spelling (`cond && 0 || 1`) always yields 1 because 0 is falsy, and on GitLab it needs `rules:`, which change when the job runs at all (both from memory, not run); and push pipelines on slice branches (D32's arm) would be left with no history to fail on. **Run by the skipper,** in scratch repositories under `/tmp/d54`, with the shipped `check-migrations.py` and the aws `check-flags.py` against a feature branch carrying an expand and contract together and a new flag seeded `on`: a developer's clone — both exit 1; a pull-request merge ref at depth 1 with no trunk ref — both exit 0; the same merge ref with all branches fetched — both exit 1; a push to the trunk at depth 1 and with full history — both exit 0. The failing runs print the two *new in this change* lines. The azure script's `declared_before()` and rule 3 were read, not run. **Read, not run:** on a depth-1 checkout `check-flags` compares with `HEAD`'s own declaration, which is silent to the same effect; with all branches fetched a pushed `master` at the pull request's head can move `check-migrations`' base to that head where the branch already contains the trunk's tip, which returns today's silent answer, never a weaker one. **From memory, not run:** what `actions/checkout@v6` fetches at depth 1 and at `fetch-depth: 0`, and that a GitLab runner with `GIT_DEPTH: "0"` fetches every branch. Constitution V's one capability per slice is kept under (a). The PRD artifact was not consulted. Not an ADR: reversing either the block or (a) is a refactor of two generator functions, one branch of `check()`, their tests and a fragment; no schema, contract, dependency or stored shape is involved.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** the owner says D39 already meant the fetch with everything it brings — then (a) stands as written above and S24 is driven at once; if they choose slice pull requests only, (b) is planned with its GitHub expression and GitLab form run on a real forge first.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S24's row and graph row: blocked on a person's approval); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (S24's gaps review, left open)
- **Status:** standing

## D55 — Can any part of S24 be built without the approval D54 waits on — behind a stub, or under a narrower reading that keeps every MUST?
- **Stage:** slice gaps (blocked; bosun) · **Slice:** S24-ci-fetches-slice-base · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:20:39Z · **Iteration:** 8
- **Question:** D54 returned `unavailable: a person's approval` for what S24's fetch does to `check-migrations` and `check-flags` in CI. By the bosun's moves in order — stub the world, narrow the reading, repair the run — is there a part of S24 that can be built now with the owner brief's *Always ask a person* and every constitution MUST intact, or does the slice wait?
- **Options:** (a) stub: build S24 as if the approval were given, or behind a fake of it; (b) narrow to the checker's half — *NOT checked* becomes exit 1 in a forge's checkout, no workflow change; (c) narrow to the workflow's half — fetch history, leave the checker's exit 0; (d) fetch behind a new default-off setting, the person turning it on; (e) give `check-slice-scope` its base under a ref name the other two gates do not read; (f) build only tests now, pinning what the two gates answer with and without history; (g) nothing is built: S24 waits for the answer to D54 and the run takes the next ready slice. The host recommended none and asked that (b)–(e) be weighed.
- **Decision:** (g) — **`cannot`**. No stub, narrower reading or repair moves S24 safely; it stays blocked on the person's answer to D54's question, which stands as written and ready to send. **This does not park the run:** `S02-runner-bookkeeping` is ready and is taken next, then `S11-render-once` (D39's order). Nothing is written to the tree for S24 and no task is added to another slice, because no stub exists to remove. When the person answers, the gaps stage is re-entered at the open note under `### S24-ci-fetches-slice-base` in `spec.md`, as D54 says.
- **Why:** What is missing is a person's consent, not a part of the world, so there is no port to put a fake behind; (a) would record an approval nobody gave as a fact, and it changes what CI checks. (b) is D31's rejected option (c): after `migrate` every slice pull request goes red for a reason the branch cannot fix. (c) is the consequence D54 is asking about, shipped without the answer — the fetch alone is what makes the two gates hold on every pull request. (d) adds a setting to a slice the split and D54 call a PATCH with *no file, option, flag or setting added* (`AGENTS.md`: a new setting is a MINOR; owner brief, *Taste*), ships a path no project runs until someone turns it on — which is never the run's — and leaves an option that must later be kept for good or removed, when the recommended answer needs no setting at all. (e) is D54's standing rejection, and it also changes how the checker chooses its base, which S24's row defers (*any change to what the checker holds*) and D30 fixes. (f) is safe but is not the slice: the gaps review has written no criterion, so tests now would pin a behaviour the person has not chosen between (D54's (a) and (b) differ on exactly those answers), and a second pull request for S24 breaks one pull request per slice. For the developer nothing is lost by waiting: CI answers exactly as it does today, the *NOT checked* line already says so and names the key, and the cost is one message to an owner who answered the last one the same day. Repair does not apply: the checkout is sound, no delegate died, no gate loops. Constitution I and V and the owner brief's first *Always ask a person* item are kept by building nothing. Not an ADR: nothing is decided that outlives the slice.
- **Decided by:** drive-bosun
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person answers D54's question — yes to the recommendation makes D54 its option (a) and S24 is driven at once; *slice pull requests only* makes it (b), planned after its forge forms are run; or the person says they want the fetch behind a setting, which reopens (d) as a MINOR.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S24's graph row: one clause)
- **Status:** standing


## D56 — What may `controls_signature()` rest on, and what does FR-024's *once per iteration from mtimes* mean when a before and an after are both needed and a person may edit a control while the run is parked?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:25:44Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** FR-024 says the runner MUST compute `controls_signature()` once per iteration from mtimes. Today `controls_signature()` in `assets/toolkit/scripts/agents/cruise.py` hashes the content of every file under the controls, and `drive()` takes it before and after each iteration and parks the run on any difference (`controls_changed()`). That comparison holds the catastrophic-list rule *making a gate pass by changing the gate*. What may the signature rest on, what does *once per iteration* mean with two signatures per iteration, and what happens to a control a person edits while the run is parked?
- **Options:** (a) the letter: path and modification time, no content read; (b) D49's precedent: the runner keeps each control file's content hash beside its stat facts, stats every file on each signature, re-hashes only a file whose record is missing or differs, and still compares content hashes; (c) leave the function as it is and say FR-024's first clause is not taken in this slice; (d) something else sound. The stage led with (b) and recommended none outright.
- **Decision:** (b), the stage's lead, taken with two things stricter than D49. D49 is not overridden; it governs `check-codegraph`, and this entry governs the runner's controls.
  - **What the signature is:** unchanged. It maps each control file's path to the SHA-256 of its bytes. `controls_changed()` and the `controls_changed` log field, the park message and the `tools/` install exemption are not touched and answer byte for byte as today.
  - **The record:** for each control file the runner keeps the content hash, size, modification time and change time in nanoseconds, identity (device and inode), and the moment the hash was taken. The stat facts are read from the file as it was opened for hashing, before its bytes are read. Stat follows a symlink, as the read does.
  - **A later signature:** it walks the same paths with the same skipped directories and stats every file. It reuses a recorded hash only where all four facts read exactly as recorded. Any other file is read and hashed, and its record renewed.
  - **Written too close to its hash:** a file whose modification or change time is not safely older than the moment it was last hashed is hashed again on every signature until it is. *Safely* means two seconds.
  - **Stricter than D49, all four or nothing:** where the platform gives no true change time or no identity (Windows, where Python's change time is the creation time; an inode of 0), nothing is reused and every file is hashed on every signature, as today. D49's *as many as the platform reports* is not taken here, because what this record is guarding against is the iteration itself.
  - **Appears, disappears, cannot be stat'ed or read:** exactly today's answers. A file not in the walk, or one whose stat or read raises, is absent from the signature and its record is dropped. So it compares as `(deleted)` if it was there before, and as `(added)` if it is there after and is not under `tools/`. A control path that does not exist contributes nothing. No record is ever kept for a file that could not be read.
  - **Before and after:** both are still taken, every iteration, each by a fresh stat of every file. The after-signature of one iteration never serves as the before of the next. Only the record carries over. A control a person edits during a park, or in the poll between iterations, differs in its stat, is re-hashed into the next before-signature, and is not held against that iteration. A change a person *keeps on purpose and resumes with a message* is likewise not reported twice.
  - **How FR-024 reads:** *once per iteration from mtimes* means each control file's content is read at most once per iteration unless its stat facts changed, and at most once per runner process while nothing changes. The comparison the run parks on stays a comparison of content.
  - **Where the record lives:** only in the runner's process, never on disk. A new runner process hashes everything once, including a `--no-park` run started again. There is no file, no setting, no key and nothing for `git status`.
  - **Unavailable:** (a). It lets an iteration edit a gate and put the modification time back with one `touch -r`, which weakens the control the catastrophic list rests on. That is the owner brief's *removes a check anywhere* and fails priority 5.
  - **Rejected:** (c). It leaves a MUST that the split places in S02 unmet when a sound reading meets it.
  - **Level:** PATCH, as S02's row says. The fragment carries one sentence on the residual below.
  - **Criteria:** numbered by the host as AC-S02-1 to AC-S02-11, under S02-runner-bookkeeping in the specification.
- **Why:** The developer sees two things: how long a slice takes and whether the gate can still be believed. This control is the second; it is the only thing on every harness that stops an iteration making a gate pass by editing it.
  - **The saving is small, so nothing may be traded for it.** Measured by the skipper on this checkout's controls (35 files, 677 kB): hashing content takes about 1 ms and stat alone about 0.2 ms. A project with extensions installed under `tools/` has more, but the point holds.
  - **(b) meets the MUST at no loss the control can feel.** Run in a scratch directory under `/tmp/d56` (Linux, tmpfs, Python 3.14.4): a same-size rewrite in place with its modification time restored kept size, mtime and inode and moved the change time. A replace by rename with the old mtime moved both change time and inode. An unprivileged process cannot set a change time, so on POSIX the residual needs the clock set back.
  - **Why all four or nothing.** D49 accepted missing facts for a developer's local index, where the harm is a stale row. Here the party is the agent being guarded, so a platform that cannot give all four keeps today's full hashing.
  - **Why the record is in the process only.** A record on disk is a file an iteration could rewrite to vouch for its own edit. It would also need a key over the script hashes and a place outside the tree. None of that buys anything when one full hash per process costs milliseconds.
  - **Why the before is always fresh.** `park()` waits inside the same process, and the page tells a person to *revert the change, or keep it on purpose and resume with a message*. Carrying the after forward would charge the person's edit to the next iteration and park a run for obeying its own instructions.
  - **Standing rules kept.** Constitution I holds: nothing at the merge root or in CI changes and no check is removed, so the owner brief's *Always ask a person* is not reached. Owner priority 2 (memoise before optimising) and priority 5 are both met.
  - **The residual, for the fragment:** *a control whose bytes were changed while its size, modification time, change time and identity all read as before — a clock set back — is not seen by that runner process; a new runner process hashes everything.*
  - **Not verified:** Windows and network filesystems (covered only by the all-four rule and the two-second clause); the cost on a project with a large `tools/` tree; that the two-second margin is enough on a filesystem with coarser timestamps than that. `fingerprint()` and FR-024's second clause (one codegraph sync per iteration) were not part of this question and are not decided here.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** an iteration is shown changing a control's bytes on an ordinary developer filesystem with size, modification time, change time and identity all unchanged and no clock moved. Then the record is unsound for this control, the function goes back to content every time, and FR-024's first clause goes to a person as unmet.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (S02's criteria, to be numbered AC-S02-1 to AC-S02-11 by the host; FR-024's reading)
- **Status:** standing


## D57 — What is the runner's fingerprint made of once it stops reading every file under `specs/` on every call (FR-015, *fingerprint by path and mtime*)?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:25:56Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** FR-015 says the runner MUST *fingerprint by path and mtime*, and the slice's example says *given an unchanged `specs/` tree, when `fingerprint()` runs, then it opens no file for content*. Today `fingerprint()` (`assets/toolkit/scripts/agents/cruise.py`, line 461) digests `git rev-parse HEAD`, a filtered `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all`, and the path and bytes of every file under `specs/` but the log and the checkpoint. The stuck detector compares that value (`drive()`, the `fingerprints` window against `stuck_after`), a parked wait polls it (`park()`), every entry of `specs/cruise-log.jsonl` carries it, and a new runner process seeds its window from the log. What is it made of after this slice: what do a touched-but-identical file, a same-size rewrite and a file added or removed answer, do the two `git` subprocesses stay, must the value equal today's for the same tree, and how is *opens no file for content* measured given a first call?
- **Options:** (a) to the letter: path and modification time, no content. (b) Content still, read once: the runner process keeps each file's content hash beside its stat record and re-reads only a file whose record is missing or differs, with the value for a given tree unchanged from today's. (c) The same as (b) with the record on disk, so a new process's first call also opens nothing. (d) Something else sound. The stage led with (b) and recommended none outright.
- **Decision:** (b), the stage's lead, with one departure: the value does **not** equal today's, because it cannot.
  - **What it is made of.** One SHA-256, cut to 16 hex characters as today, over three things in this order: the output of `git rev-parse HEAD`; the filtered `git status` lines exactly as today; and, for every file under `specs/` but the log and the checkpoint, in sorted order, its path relative to the root and the SHA-256 of its bytes.
  - **The record.** The runner process keeps, for each such file, its content hash beside its size, modification time and change time in nanoseconds, and its identity (inode) — as many of the four as the platform reports, taken from the file as it was opened for hashing, before its bytes were read. This is D49's record, in memory. A later call stats every file and opens only one whose record is missing or differs in any field. A file whose modification or change time is not safely older than the moment it was read (the filesystem's granularity; where that is unknown, two seconds) is read again on the next call. A file that is read and found equal has its record renewed. A file no longer in the tree has its record dropped.
  - **Touched but identical** (`touch`, a `git checkout`, a rewrite with the same bytes): read once, hash equal, fingerprint unchanged. It is not progress.
  - **Same-size rewrite:** its times move, it is read, the hash differs, the fingerprint changes. With the modification time restored, the change time still moves it.
  - **Added, removed or renamed:** the fingerprint changes, because the path is in the digest. An added file is read once.
  - **The `git` subprocesses stay**, both, with the same arguments and the same filter. They are two per call whatever the iteration, and they are the only thing that sees a commit or a change outside `specs/`.
  - **The value changes, once.** Today's value streams every file's bytes through one digest, and no per-file record can reproduce that without holding or re-reading every byte. So a window seeded from entries the present code wrote never equals a new value. In a run that was already spinning when the runner was replaced, the stuck detector parks up to `stuck_after − 1` iterations later, once. It can never park early, because a false *stuck* needs equal values. The entry's field name and 16-hex shape do not change, nothing but `drive()` reads the field, and old entries are not rewritten.
  - **Measurement.** *Opens no file for content* is about the second and later calls of one runner process, over a tree whose files were last written longer ago than the granularity window. It is counted by a fake reader written in the test tree at the read seam, not by timing. The first call of a process opens each file exactly once. SC-006 for `fingerprint()` compares a later call against the second, not the first.
  - **Rejected.** (a): a run that rewrites the same bytes every iteration would never park, and the stuck detector exists to catch that run. (c): a second file to keep out of the fingerprint, out of git and sound across a `migrate`, to save one read of `specs/` per runner process.
  - **Accepted residual, for the fragment:** *a file under `specs/` whose bytes changed while its size, modification time, change time and identity all read as before — a clock set back, a filesystem that keeps no such times — is not seen by that runner process; a commit, any other change, a person's message or a new runner process sees it.*
  - **FR-015 and scenario 2 as amended:** *the runner MUST fingerprint `specs/` by each file's path and content, opening a file only where its path is new or its size, modification time, change time or identity differ from what the runner recorded when it last read it.* User Story 5's sentence and the `story-split.md` row follow.
  - **Criteria:** numbered by the host as AC-S02-12 to AC-S02-21, under S02-runner-bookkeeping in the specification.
- **Why:** The developer leaves a run unattended and relies on it parking when it stops moving. Under (a) an iteration that checks out, touches or regenerates a file with the same bytes resets the window every time, and the run spends sessions until the budget ends. The owner brief's priority 2 asks for *fewer runs of the same check on the same content*, which is (b) exactly: the content is still what is compared, and it is read once. Priority 5 puts deterministic over fast, and D49 already settled which evidence a skipped read may rest on, so this slice uses the same four facts and the same written-during-the-run clause rather than a weaker rule. The record stays in memory because one runner process spans the whole run (`drive()` loops and `park()` polls in it), so the first-call read is paid once per run. The cost that grows is the per-iteration and per-poll re-read of `specs/`, 138 files and 2.1 MB here today. Constitution principle I is untouched, since the fingerprint is not a gate and nothing at the merge root or in CI changes. Principle VIII holds: the log's schema is unchanged and the reader tolerates old values. Nothing on the brief's *always ask a person* list is involved, and the level stays the PATCH the split gave the runner. The stage's claim that (b) keeps today's value was checked against the code and does not hold; the delay it costs is bounded and errs toward running, not toward parking.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a runner is shown, on an ordinary developer filesystem with no clock moved, returning an unchanged fingerprint for changed bytes under `specs/`. Then the record is unsound here, the content is read on every call as today, and scenario 2 goes to a person as unmet.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (FR-015 and User Story 5 scenario 2 as amended; the AC-S02-12 to AC-S02-21 lines above, numbered by the host); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S02's row, the example's wording); the slice's changelog fragment (the residual and the one-off value change)
- **Status:** standing


## D58 — Where does the runner's log offset live, what does it cover, and what does a log or stream that is not the one the runner last saw answer?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:28:24Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** FR-015 says the runner MUST tail its logs from a stored offset, and S02's first example says that at iteration 51 of a 50-iteration log `entries()` reads only the new bytes and its time is within 20 % of iteration 1. Today `drive()` in `assets/toolkit/scripts/agents/cruise.py` reads and parses the whole of `specs/cruise-log.jsonl` once to seed the stuck window and up to twice more per iteration for the number, and `code_index.delegate_use(STREAM, iteration)` reads the whole of `.specify/cruise-stream.jsonl` to find one iteration's lines. Unstated: (1) where the offset lives and which readers it covers; (2) what a truncated, replaced, edited, deleted or half-written log answers; (3) whether the iteration number stays the count of entries; (4) how *only the new bytes* and *within 20 %* are measured without a timing flake.
- **Options:** (a) the offset lives in the runner's process, valid only while the file is exactly what the runner left, with a whole re-read otherwise; the stream is read from the byte the runner wrote the marker at; the on-demand verbs keep reading whole files. (b) the offset is a file under `.specify/`, so a new runner process and the on-demand verbs also tail. (c) something else. The stage leaned to (a) and recommended none outright.
- **Decision:** (a), the stage's lead, taken after reading the code, with four additions: a stricter validity rule, a rule for edits made during an iteration, the marker as the stream's own check, and a byte count in the log entry as the measurement.
  - **Runner bookkeeping, which SC-006 measures:** the three reads of the log in `drive()` (the seed of the stuck window, and the two `len(entries()) + 1` reads) and the one `delegate_use(STREAM, iteration)` call. `fingerprint()`, `controls_signature()` and `code_index.health()` are bookkeeping too, but they are the slice's other examples and are not decided here.
  - **Not runner bookkeeping, and unchanged byte for byte:** `resume`, `tell`, `status` (with `refusals()`, `index_use_lines()`, `delegate_lines()`), `denials` and `where` read whole files once per invocation; `watch` keeps its own cursor on the run log.
  - **The log's offset:** it is what the runner's process remembers after its own append — the entry count, plus the file's size, modification time, change time and identity (D49's four facts, as many as the platform reports), taken from the handle it appended through.
  - **Start of a process:** one whole read, exactly once, serves both the stuck window and the first number.
  - **Later iterations:** the runner stats the log. If all recorded facts are unchanged it reads no byte, because the only new entry is the one it wrote. If any differs, or the file is gone, it forgets what it remembered and reads the whole file as today.
  - **Bytes appended by another hand are not tailed.** A stat cannot show the earlier bytes are untouched, so a grown file is a whole read.
  - **Changed during an iteration:** the runner checks the facts again immediately before appending. If the log is not as it left it, it appends as today, remembers nothing, and the next read is whole.
  - **What a changed log answers:** always what today's whole read answers.
    - Truncated: the number restarts from the entries left.
    - Deleted: iteration 1, and the append recreates the file.
    - Replaced, even with identical bytes, or edited in place: one whole re-read, the same count.
    - A last line that does not parse: the run ends on the parse error as it does today and appends nothing. A one-line message in its place is an older defect for the Parking Lot (D39's order).
  - **Iteration number:** it stays the count of entries plus one, so the runner, `status` and `where` keep agreeing.
  - **Stuck window:** it stays as today — seeded once per process and kept in memory, not re-seeded by a re-read.
  - **The stream's offset:** the byte at which the runner wrote this iteration's `# iteration <n>` marker. The entry's `index_use` is computed from that byte on with the same parser. The offset is used only where the path is still the file the runner wrote through and the bytes at the offset are exactly the marker line it wrote. Otherwise today's whole read for that iteration runs, and a deleted stream gives no `index_use`, as today. Whether the runner re-reads its section or counts as it writes is the plan's.
  - **Measurement:** each entry the runner writes carries a new optional `bookkeeping` object with `log_bytes` and `stream_bytes`. `log_bytes` is the bytes the runner read from the log since its previous append, or since the process started. `stream_bytes` is the bytes it read from the stream for this entry. The tests assert these counts, read back through the log as the runner's tests already do. No test asserts a wall-clock ratio; SC-006's 20 % for these two reads is held as *the bytes read do not depend on how long the history is*, and a timing goes in the demo as evidence only.
  - **Version level:** the field is a new thing an entry may carry, inside the MINOR the slice already claims for `Scope:`. The fragment names it.
  - **Residual, stated and not coded:** a log rewritten at the same size with all four facts reading as before is not seen until the next runner process. D49's two-second rule is not applied, because the runner stats milliseconds after its own append and the rule would force a whole read every iteration.
  - **(b) is rejected; nothing is unavailable.**
  - **Criteria:** numbered by the host as AC-S02-22 to AC-S02-33, under S02-runner-bookkeeping in the specification.
- **Why:** The developer sees how long a slice takes and whether the run can be believed.
  - **Why in-process.** The runner is the only writer of the log: `record()` is its one appender and the session only reads the number. So once a process has read the log, the new bytes at each iteration are its own entry. A new process reads the file once per run, not once per iteration, which is where the growth was.
  - **Why not (b).** An offset file would be a new generated file needing its own stale and hand-edited answers and an ignore line. It would also persist an inode and timestamps, against the constitution's *persisted data MUST record facts true on any machine*. And it would buy only that one read per process (owner *Taste*; constitution III).
  - **Why whole re-read on any difference.** Owner priority 5 puts deterministic over fast. The log is tracked by git here, and a session can check out, rebase or edit it during an iteration that lasts hours. That is why the check before the append matters: without it the runner would record the changed file as its own and carry a wrong count for the rest of the run.
  - **Why the residual is acceptable.** A wrong count costs a reused iteration number, never a false green. That is why D49's facts are reused but its two-second rule is not.
  - **Why the marker check.** It costs one line's compare and makes the stream's offset self-proving.
  - **Why a field in the entry.** The runner's tests run it as a subprocess against a fake harness and read the log back, so a count has to be said by the runner. D50 refused a setting or seam added for tests' sake. The entry already carries the runner's own overhead as `index` with its seconds, and a field lets SC-006 be read off any real run (owner priority 6). A feed line per iteration would be noise for the person watching.
  - **Why not wall-clock.** A wall-clock ratio in a test is the flake the constitution calls a defect.
  - **Found and not decided here:** `status` reads the stream three times in one invocation (`refusals()`, `index_use_lines()`, `delegate_lines()`). It is on demand and not runner bookkeeping; a Parking Lot line under priority 2.
  - **Checked by reading, not run:** every caller of `entries()` (lines 813, 1088, 1197, 1227, 1228, 1620, 1669) and every reader of `STREAM` (lines 769, 1259, 1525, 1551, 1576, 1587). `.gitignore` ignores the stream and not the log. No code reads the log entry against a fixed set of keys. A line that does not parse raises out of `entries()` uncaught. `tests/test_cruise_runner.py` drives the runner as a subprocess.
  - **Not verified:** change time and identity on Windows and network filesystems, covered only by *as many as the platform reports*.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person does not want a `bookkeeping` field in `specs/cruise-log.jsonl` — then the two counts go on a feed line and the criteria read the run log instead; or a second writer of the log is found or added, in which case a grown file must be tailed with a check on the earlier bytes, not re-read. (High on the in-process offset and the whole re-read; medium on the field's name and on leaving the half-written line as today.)
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (S02's gaps review and its criteria, numbered by the host); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S02's row: the first example reworded to the byte counts; Parking Lot: the unparseable log line, status reading the stream three times)
- **Status:** standing


## D59 — What does S02 deliver for *drift-check only changed files* (FR-015) and *one codegraph sync per iteration unless a delegate reports changed files* (FR-024), when the runner's one drift check is `health()` and it runs on any branch?

- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:26:54Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** FR-015 says the runner MUST drift-check only changed files; FR-024 says one codegraph sync per iteration unless a delegate reports changed files. The split's row for S02 carries no example for either and defers result contracts to `S14`, which is how a delegate would report changed files. The runner's one drift check is `health()` in `assets/toolkit/scripts/agents/code_index.py`, called once before every iteration from `drive()` in `agents/cruise.py`; through `behind()` it calls the gate's `drift()` with no arguments, so every tracked file is hashed every iteration. S01 gave the gate a memory and a narrowed comparison (D46, D49, D50), which the gate's own `main()` uses only on a `slice/<id>` branch outside CI. The runner runs on any branch, the trunk included. What does this slice deliver for the two clauses, and may a narrowed `health()` write the gate's memory?
- **Options:** (a) `health()` asks the gate for its narrowed comparison wherever the runner runs — same memory, same candidates, same *cannot tell means the whole comparison* — with the sync clause pinned as a criterion and its *unless a delegate reports* half deferred to `S14`; (b) `health()` keeps a memory of its own; (c) neither clause is taken here, both wait for `S14`; (d) something else. The stage led with (a) and left the question of writing the memory open.
- **Decision:** (a), the stage's lead, with the writing question settled: `health()` may write and renew the memory, through the gate's own writer and under the gate's own rules.
  - **Where it narrows.** `health()` narrows on every branch (trunk, `slice/<id>`, any other, detached `HEAD`) wherever no CI marker is set and the memory is usable. With a CI marker (`CI`, `GITHUB_ACTIONS`, `GITLAB_CI`) it compares everything, and the memory is neither read nor written (D46 rule 8).
  - **How it compares.** `health()` uses the gate's own functions and never a second implementation: the candidates of D46 rules 2 to 5 and D49, one read of the index's rows per comparison (D53's F1), and the no-row judgement of D50. The comparison after its one sync is made the same way.
  - **Integrity check.** `health()` keeps running SQLite's integrity check on every call, narrowed or not. `health()` is the thing that repairs a corrupt database, and FR-015 does not ask for the check to go. A rebuilt database is *not the one it was compared against*, so the comparison after a rebuild is whole.
  - **Where the memory cannot be used.** `health()` does today's whole comparison and says why in one clause of its `detail`. This covers no memory, an unreadable one, changed gate scripts, a gone commit, another database, git unable to say what changed, an index that will not hold still or cannot be read, and any exception from reading or using the memory. It is never `failed` for that reason alone and never `current` on less than the whole comparison.
  - **Writing the memory.** A `health()` comparison that ends current, whole or narrowed, straight away or after its one sync, writes or renews the memory exactly as a passing gate run does. A `health()` that ends `failed` or `unreachable`, cannot open the database, or gets no answer from the comparison writes nothing. This extends *a run* in D46's rule 1 to the runner's comparison; it overrides nothing, and D46, D49 and D50 stay standing.
  - **What the gate does.** Nothing changes. `narrowable()` and `main()` are untouched; `make verify` on the trunk, on any branch not shaped `slice/<id>` and in CI hashes every tracked file and runs the integrity check as before; the gate's verdict is never formed from `health()`'s comparison, including where `whole()` calls `health()` to rebuild a corrupt database. **No option taken here changes what the merge root or CI checks, so no person is needed.** D46's *Unavailable* (narrowing the gate on the trunk) stands and is not touched.
  - **The sync clause.** The runner invokes `codegraph sync` at most once per iteration, before the iteration starts, and only where the comparison found files behind. That is true today and becomes a pinned criterion.
  - **Deferred to `S14`, in words the split can carry:** *a further sync inside an iteration tied to the changed files a delegate reports in its result contract, in place of the unconditional sync after every delegate's return; and handing those reported files to the runner's comparison as candidates (→ `S14`).* The harness's `PostToolUse` sync after each delegate is unchanged in S02.
  - **Unavailable:** nothing. (b) and (c) are available and rejected.
  - **Criteria:** numbered by the host as AC-S02-34 to AC-S02-46, under S02-runner-bookkeeping in the specification.
- **Why:** The developer cruising a repository pays `health()` before every iteration, over every tracked file, although nearly all of them are the bytes the last comparison already vouched for. That is the *same check on the same content* the owner's priority 2 says to memoise first. FR-015 names the runner, and the runner runs on the trunk, so the owner's own words already cover narrowing there, as FR-022 did for the gate in D46.
  - **Not a gate.** `health()` has no exit code in `make verify`, fails nobody's build, and its job is to ready an index for an iteration. Constitution I (a scoped or memoised gate is additive; no check removed anywhere) and the brief's *Always ask a person* are about what the merge root and CI check, and that stays whole. No comparison is removed: where the memory cannot vouch, the whole comparison runs.
  - **What a wrong `current` costs.** One iteration's delegates ask an index with a stale row and may be told *nothing calls that*, then build on it. The cost is rework in converge. It never puts a bad change on `main`, because the tests and the gate judge the code and not the index.
  - **What still catches it.** A wrong `current` can only come from D49's accepted residual (bytes changed with size, both times and identity all reading as before). The harness's sync after each delegate returns brings the index up regardless of what `health()` said. The next whole comparison sees it: `make verify` on the trunk or any branch not shaped `slice/<id>`, or any run after the memory is deleted or the gate's scripts change. On a `slice/<id>` branch the gate narrows from the same memory and shares the blind spot. CI normally has no index, so it is not the catch.
  - **Why `health()` may write.** D46's rule 1 asks that the memory be written only after a comparison that found the index current. A passing `health()` is that, by the gate's own functions, and it has also run the integrity check a narrowed gate skips. A reader that could not write would hash a touched or freshly synced file on every iteration until some gate run renewed it, and would need a second "compare but do not remember" path to keep correct.
  - **Why not (b) or (c).** (b) is a second cache with a second set of rules for the same question, each needing its own D49; owner priority 5 is better served by one. (c) leaves SC-006 unmet for the one part of the runner's cost that grows with the repository, although nothing in the narrowing needs a result contract.
  - **Verified by reading:** `health()`, `behind()` and `gate()` in `code_index.py`; `drift()`, `remembered()`, `candidates_of()`, `unvouched()`, `read_once()`, `narrowed()`, `remember()`, `whole()` and `main()` in `check-codegraph.py`; the call in `drive()` in `cruise.py` (line 1231), which is the only caller besides the `health` and `session` verbs and the gate's corrupt-database repair; `sync()`, which is the harness hook and syncs unconditionally.
  - **Not verified:** no reproduction was run and this tree has no index to open. The memory's key already carries the hash of `agents/code_index.py`, so this slice's own edit makes the first comparison after it whole.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person says the runner's check before an iteration on the trunk counts as something the merge root checks. Then `health()` narrows only where the gate does (a `slice/<id>` branch outside CI), and FR-015's clause on the trunk is theirs to restate.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (S02's criteria, numbered by the host); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (S02's row: the examples and the *Defers* wording for S14)
- **Status:** standing


## D60 — What is a decision's `Scope:`, what does the checker hold of it, and how does the skipper receive only the entries in scope?
- **Stage:** slice gaps · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T17:28:05Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** FR-016 says decision entries MUST carry a `Scope:` line and the skipper MUST be briefed with in-scope and global decisions only. User Story 5 says *scope intersects the slice's neighbourhood*. The slice's row gives two examples and calls the line a MINOR. Seven things are unstated: (1) what a value is and the word for global; (2) where the line sits and what `check-decisions` refuses; (3) what *in scope* means for the slice being decided; (4) what an entry with no line is to the filter; (5) whether non-standing entries are carried; (6) how the skipper receives the subset; (7) which writers add the line in this slice.
- **Options:** (a) slice ids or `global`; no line means global; in scope is the slice's own id; standing entries only; a new verb prints the subset; the entry shape and both briefs write the line; the checker accepts absence and refuses an empty value — the stage's lead, not an outright recommendation. (b) as (a), plus the slice's `depends_on` neighbours from the split. (c) scope by service or bounded context. (d) something else sound.
- **Decision:** (a), with the seven answered as follows; the stage's lead is taken, and one thing is added to it (a note, not a refusal, for a later entry that lacks the line).
  1. **Value.** `Scope:` is the one word `global`, or one or more slice ids separated by commas, written bare. Backticks around an id are tolerated. The writer names every slice whose later decisions must agree with this one: the slice that raised it and any slice the Decision names or hands work to. A feature-level decision is `global`, and so is any decision the writer is unsure of. Not a service, context or path.
  2. **Place and checker.** The shape shows `- **Scope:** …` on its own line directly after the Stage line. `check-decisions` accepts an entry with no line. Where the line is present it refuses two things, each with one line naming the entry: an empty value, and a value that is neither `global` alone nor a list of id-shaped tokens (the id shape `done_slices()` already reads). It does not check that an id names a slice that exists, and it does not hold the line's position; the first `Scope` line in an entry is the one read. Where an entry has no line and an earlier entry in the same log has one, it prints a `note:` and still passes.
  3. **In scope.** The slice's own id. Two ids match when they are equal or when one is the other's bare prefix (`S02` and `S02-runner-bookkeeping`, D19's reading); `S1` does not match `S12`. Neighbours from the Slice graph wait for `S13-one-hop-brief`. Until then a decision reaches a neighbour because its writer named that neighbour.
  4. **No line.** Carried as global, always. An entry whose line the filter cannot read is also carried as global; the filter never drops what it cannot place.
  5. **Status.** Only `standing` entries are carried. The closing line names each overridden entry by number with what overrode it.
  6. **Means.** A verb of `check-decisions.py`, which already owns the entry parser: `python3 scripts/check-decisions.py --scope <slice-id>`, with `--feature <name>` required only when `specs/` holds more than one `decisions.md`. It prints the carried entries verbatim in number order. It ends with one line saying how many it carried of how many (in scope, global, carried as global for want of a line) and what it left out (out of scope by count, overridden by number). It exits 0 when no entry names the slice. It changes nothing. The skipper's and the bosun's standing briefs, and the two sentences of the cruise command that say *read every standing entry*, point at it for a question that names a slice. A feature-level question still reads the whole log. The host does not paste entries.
  7. **Writers, in this slice.** `DECISION_ENTRY` in `src/slipwai/project/cruise_record.py`, which the cruise command and the seeded owner brief both print; the skipper's and bosun's briefs in `src/slipwai/project/cruise_agents.py`; the command's *Deciding* text, which covers host-written and `human` entries; and the checker's docstring. Making absence a failing finding is not in this slice: it is a new refusal in a gate CI runs, so it is a person's to approve (D54), and goes to the Parking Lot as that question.
  
  **Release.** MINOR. `VERSION` goes from `1.5.2.dev0` to `1.6.0.dev0` in the commit that adds the line, with a fragment claiming MINOR. Its catch-up paragraph says nothing is asked of an existing log, that older entries are carried as global, and that an owner brief the project owns may be given the line by hand. S03's graph note (*raises `VERSION` to `1.6.0.dev0`*) becomes *already there*. By D9 this repository's own log gains no `Scope:` lines until a person runs `migrate`; this entry carries none for that reason.
- **Why:** A developer cruising at iteration 50 wants the skipper to read four entries and not a hundred, and wants the hundredth answer never to contradict the fourth. Every choice above errs toward the second.
  - **`Scope:` does not duplicate `Slice:`.** Of this log's 55 entries, 11 say `none (feature-level)`, and several carry prose (`S12-model-sidecar (named ahead)`, `S00-run-path (found while deciding D12)`). `Slice:` records where the question was raised. D13 and D17 were raised in S00 and bind S20 and every later adversary row. D10 was raised at the split and binds S12. A filter reading `Slice:` would hide D17 from every slice it governs.
  - **Slice ids, not contexts.** The brief names a slice, D17 and D19 already fix how an id is read, and a factory repository has one deployable and no contexts to scope by.
  - **Own id only.** I found no script under `assets/toolkit/scripts/` that reads the split's `## Slice graph`; only agents read it, and its cells carry prose (`S01`–`S06`, *(the owner's order, D39)*). Parsing it here would add a second mechanism to a slice that already carries three requirements, and FR-014's one-hop briefing has its own slice (owner priority 4, smaller slices).
  - **No line means global.** Owner priority 5: a filter that could hide a binding decision is wrong. A missing line costs tokens; a wrong exclusion costs consistency.
  - **A verb of the checker.** One parser means the gate and the filter cannot disagree about what an entry is. `--adversary-baseline` is the precedent for a second verb, and it needs no new dependency or setting. The host pasting entries would pay for them twice.
  - **The two refusals are not D54's case.** They can fall only on a line this release introduces, and the row itself says the checker learns the line. No log written by any released factory changes its answer. D54's refusals fell on content projects already have, against the row's own promise.
  - **The line is safe for older checkers.** I read `entries()` and `check_decisions()`: a label outside `DECISION_FIELDS` is ignored today, so an older checker passes a log that carries the line (constitution VIII, readers tolerate unknown fields). I did not run it.
  - **Constitution.** No MUST is touched; principle I holds because the full gate still runs and nothing existing is refused. The PRD artifact was not consulted.
- **Decided by:** drive-skipper (claude-fable-5-1)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** the owner says the skipper must also see `depends_on` neighbours' decisions before `S13` lands — then (b), with a reader of the Slice graph added here — or says even the two refusals on a present line need their approval, in which case the checker notes them and the filter's carry-as-global is the only guard.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (S02's criteria, AC-S02-47 to AC-S02-69); `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot: absence as a finding, a person's approval; S03's VERSION note)
- **Status:** standing


## D61 — Does AC-S02-68's *that file is unchanged* exempt a seeded owner brief from `migrate`'s merge?
- **Stage:** implementation · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T18:20:07Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** T011's delegate handed back: AC-S02-68 says that when `slipwai migrate` runs on a project whose owner brief an earlier factory seeded, the file is unchanged (D24). `migrate` is a three-way merge, so a brief the project never edited would take the new `Scope:` line in its entry example from the merge. Is the criterion an exemption from the merge, or the project's text surviving it?
- **Options:** (a) the project's text survives: a refresh never writes the file, `migrate` merges the factory's new default beside the project's change, a brief nobody edited takes the new shape — what D24 already says; (b) `migrate` leaves the file byte for byte, edited or not — a change to what `migrate` writes, which D24 ruled out (*`generate`, `add-service` and `migrate` are untouched*).
- **Decision:** (a). AC-S02-68 is reworded to *what the project wrote in that file survives*, and the fragment's catch-up paragraph says the same: a refresh never rewrites the brief, `migrate` merges the new shape beside the project's own text, and where a merge leaves the example without the line it may be added by hand.
- **Why:** D24 keeps the four seeded files in `.written` precisely so that a newer factory's default reaches them through `migrate` beside the project's change; an exemption would stop the new entry shape reaching the one page the skipper reads before every decision.
- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D24)
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** a person wants the owner brief out of `migrate`'s merge altogether, which is D24's to reopen.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S02-68), `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S02-runner-bookkeeping/tasks.md` (T013's fragment wording, through the delegate's brief)
- **Status:** standing


## D62 — Where does User Story 8 (provisional approval of easily reversible decisions) go in the split?
- **Stage:** split (a person's scope through `/cruise-tell`, read at the boundary after implementation) · **Slice:** none (feature-level) · **When:** 2026-10-03T18:35:05Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** The owner committed User Story 8 to the specification at `3927c3a` (FR-029 to FR-033, SC-011) and sent, in their words: *Owner scope addition: spec.md gained User Story 8 (opportunistic optimistic approval: reversibility score per decision, decide: provisional setting, ratify/revert, dependency tracking; FR-029 to FR-033). Append it to the split as slices behind the gate slices (after S06-scoped-gate's row, before the merge tree), MINOR. The PRD doc has the matching E9. Do not re-open finished slices for it.* How is it cut and where does it sit?
- **Options:** (a) one slice for the whole story; (b) three slices along what an owner can use on its own — the score on every entry, the setting that acts on it, ratify and revert with the dependency ids — placed where the owner said; (c) behind every PRD slice, with the other additions.
- **Decision:** (b), placed as sent. `S26-reversibility-line` (FR-029), `S27-provisional-decisions` (FR-030, FR-033) and `S28-ratify-revert` (FR-031, FR-032) follow `S06-scoped-gate`'s row and precede the merge tree; each is a MINOR; none re-opens a finished slice, and S02, in flight when the message came, is finished as planned. **The host's reading, not the owner's words:** the cut into three, their names, and `S28` depending on `S14-result-contract` because FR-032 names the result contract — where that makes `S28` wait past its row, the graph governs. The run never sets `decide: provisional` itself: a setting changes only through `/cruise-settings`.
- **Why:** The owner's message and the specification they committed; three slices because each scenario group can be shown to an actor alone (owner brief, priority 4: smaller slices).
- **Decided by:** human
- **Confidence:** high · **Would reverse if:** the owner wants one slice, another order, or `S28` cut so it does not wait on `S14`.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (the three rows, the Slice graph, the Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing


## D63 — Where do the adversary's findings on S02 go, other than A2 (A1, A3–A5, B1–B3, C1–C10), with the hand's notes and T020–T022?
- **Stage:** adversary · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T20:15:06Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** Three seams returned eighteen findings. New with the slice: A1 `HIGH` (a FIFO left at `.specify/cruise-stream.jsonl` hangs the runner before it compares the controls or writes the entry; the earlier runner parks naming the edited gate), B1 `LOW` (the same path hangs `status`), B2 `LOW` (`health()` writes a record on an index with no rows, which no gate run would), B3 `LOW` (*compared everything* said where nothing was compared), C1 and C2 `HIGH` (a `Scope:` naming the slice in another spelling — a shortened slug, `S2`, lower case — or a range `S01-S03` passes the gate and is left out of `--scope`), C3 to C6 `MEDIUM` (non-ASCII digits; a `--scope` value or an argument the script does not know answered with exit 0 and no entries; an entry under a malformed heading dropped by the verb), C7 to C10 `LOW` (a second `Status` or `Scope` line wins or loses silently — the hand's note and T022; separators other than a line feed; a traceback on a log that is not UTF-8; trailing blank lines not printed). Older, identical before the slice: A3 `MEDIUM` (a FIFO at the log hangs the append), A4 and A5 `LOW` (a harness line shaped like the stream's marker, or bytes that are not UTF-8, end the runner; a file name under `specs/` that is not UTF-8 ends the run). What is fixed now and what waits?
- **Options:** (a) fix in this slice, each through a failing test, everything new with it that breaks something it promised, closing each class; older findings behind the PRD's slices unless they share a class being closed — the ladder's rule and D53's practice; (b) a defect slice for the `Scope:` findings; (c) lines only.
- **Decision:** (a). **Fixed now:** A1 with B1, and A3 because it is the same class — a path the runner reads or appends to as its own is opened only where it is a regular file, and the controls are compared and named before anything that can block; B2 and B3; C1 to C9 with T022 by one rule that follows D60's own (*the filter never drops what it cannot place*): ids meet on their head — the letters and the number, the number read as a number, case set aside — whatever the slug; a value the checker cannot read as `global` alone or a list of single ids in ASCII is refused by the gate and carried as global by the verb; an entry that says its scope or its status twice is refused by the gate and carried by the verb; a block under a heading the checker cannot read is printed and counted, and the verb then exits 1 saying the log does not pass the gate; a `--scope` value that is not id-shaped, and any argument the script does not know, is usage and exit 2, never a green run of something else; a log that is not UTF-8 is one line naming it. T020 and T021 ride along. **This amends D60's answer 3 in one respect:** two ids meet when their heads are equal, not only when one is the other's bare prefix — `S02-runner` and `S2` meet `S02-runner-bookkeeping`; `S1` still does not meet `S12`. **Not changed:** C10 (blank lines at an entry's end are not printed; no content is lost). **To the Parking Lot, behind the PRD's slices (D39):** A4 and A5, older and `LOW`, and the hand's notes 4, 5 and 7 (an uncommitted file is hashed on every `health()`; no clause under a CI marker; the harness named on an entry under `CRUISE_HARNESS_COMMAND`). The hand's note 3 (the closing line names every overridden entry) stands as D60 wrote it. A2 is D64's.
- **Why:** A1 lets an iteration that edited a gate stop the run from ever saying so — the slice's own regression in the one control it re-decided. C1 and C2 are what an ordinary writer produces (the split's own cells carry ranges), and the skipper would then decide in ignorance of an entry that binds it, which is the one failure D60 was written to prevent. The rest are a few lines each in files the fixes already open. The older `LOW` ones do not touch what the slice promised, and the owner put those behind the PRD's slices.
- **Decided by:** host (standing decision D60)
- **Confidence:** medium · **Would reverse if:** a person wants ids to meet only when spelled alike — then a near-miss is a gate finding and nothing is carried on its head — or wants A3 left as it always was.
- **Written to:** `specs/001-faster-slipwai/spec.md` (AC-S02-80 to AC-S02-88), `specs/001-faster-slipwai/slices/S02-runner-bookkeeping/tasks.md` (T024 to T027), `specs/001-faster-slipwai/adversary-log.md` (S02's row), `specs/001-faster-slipwai/story-split.md` (Parking Lot)
- **Status:** standing


## D64 — What grade is the adversary's A2 (a process an iteration leaves behind edits a gate between two iterations, unseen by the run), where does it go, and what does the runner do about it?
- **Stage:** adversary · **Slice:** S02-runner-bookkeeping · **When:** 2026-10-03T20:13:16Z · **Iteration:** 9
- **Question:** The runner takes a signature of its controls before and after every iteration and parks on a difference. A process the iteration starts in a session of its own (`setsid … &`) survives the runner's `killpg`, waits for the iteration's log entry (written after the after-signature) and then edits a gate. The next before-signature is taken fresh (D56), so the edit becomes the baseline: no park, no `controls_changed`, and later iterations run against the changed gate. It reproduces on this tip and identically at `596740f`, before the slice. What is its grade, is it fixed in this slice or placed behind the PRD's slices (D39), and if fixed, what exactly does the runner do?
- **Options:** (a) fix now: the runner keeps the after-signature and compares it with the next before-signature; a difference with no park between parks the run, a difference across a park is named and not parked on; (b) the same, but across a park the run parks again until a person confirms with a message; (c) not this slice: a defect slice behind the PRD's slices at `HIGH`, or the next slice at `CRITICAL`; (d) something else, such as ending the iteration's whole session rather than its process group. The stage led with (a) at `HIGH` and recommended none outright.
- **Decision:** (a), the stage's lead, graded `HIGH` and fixed in S02's Phase 4 through failing tests.
  - **Grade: `HIGH`, not `CRITICAL`.** The standing definition (`delivery/commands/adversary.md`, and every earlier row of the log) is *data of one actor reaches another, or an actor gains a role*. Neither happens: the iteration's shell could always write the file, and what it evades for one window is the runner's detection. The host's line to the adversary (*an iteration changes a gate unseen*) is not a standing rule and does not change the definition. The grade changes nothing done here, because the finding is closed before the slice ships either way.
  - **Where it goes: this slice.** D39 is not overridden: it places defect *slices* behind the PRD's, and this opens none. D53's practice (older findings wait) is departed from for this one finding, because what is new with the slice is the written promise. D56 says this control is the one thing on every harness that stops an iteration editing a gate, and the fragment's *what it cannot see* names only a clock set back. Shipping (c) ships both sentences known to be incomplete.
  - **What the runner keeps.** The after-signature of the last iteration this runner process ran, and whether `park()` has returned since. Both live in the process only, never on disk (D56's reason stands).
  - **What is compared.** At the start of every iteration but the process's first, the kept after-signature is compared with this iteration's before-signature, using `controls_changed()` as it is. The before-signature is still taken fresh, where it is taken today, and is still the only thing the iteration is charged against. It is one signature serving both comparisons, so no window opens between the check and the session starting.
  - **`tools/`.** The same exemption as within an iteration, because the same function answers: a file added under `tools/` is an install and is not a change; one modified or deleted there is.
  - **What parks.** A difference found where no park came between. This covers the ordinary boundary, an iteration ended by `tell --now`, and the bosun's one unblocking iteration. The run parks before the iteration starts, with: *a gate or a control of the run changed between iterations {N−1} and {N} — {files} — and nothing an iteration starts may change one; revert the change, or keep it on purpose and resume with a message*. No log entry is written for the park, the iteration number is not consumed, and a person's message already taken for that iteration still rides on the iteration that eventually runs.
  - **What is only named.** A difference found where a park came between, including the park just described once it is resumed. The run does not park on it (D56 and AC-S02-6 stand). The feed gets one line starting `cruise:`, naming the files and saying they changed while the run was parked and that iteration N starts against them.
  - **The field.** `controls_changed_between`, on the entry of the iteration that started against the difference. It is a list of strings in `controls_changed`'s form (`path (modified)`, `(deleted)`, `(added)`), optional, absent when empty, and nothing reads it back but a person. It is the net difference at that iteration's start: a change reverted during the park leaves no field (the park line in the feed tells it), and a change kept leaves the field.
  - **Not reported twice.** An iteration that changed a gate, was parked for it, and whose change a person kept: the next before equals the kept after, so nothing is named (D56's clause holds). If the person reverted it, the next entry names the file `(modified)`, which is true.
  - **`--no-park`.** A between-iterations difference ends the run with exit 3 and the park line as its last output, as a change during an iteration does. Nothing is in the log for it. The next runner process has no kept signature and compares nothing at its first iteration.
  - **A person's `tell` during a park.** It resumes the run as today. It is not required as a confirmation and is not read as one: the difference is named however the park ended, and the message sits in `told` on the same entry.
  - **D56's sentence, amended by one clause.** *The after-signature of one iteration never serves as the before of the next* stands. Added: *it is compared with it, so no control changes between two iterations silently.*
  - **Not taken: (b).** The runner cannot tell a person from a process running as the same user. A left-behind process can write the inbox as easily as it can touch a file under `specs/`. So a required message stops no one who means to get past it, and it parks a person a second time for following the page's own instruction.
  - **Not taken: (d).** Ending the whole session is not possible without privilege on both platforms. A descendant that calls `setsid` has left the session and the group. Linux could hold it with a cgroup, a PID namespace or a child subreaper, none of which macOS has. A job handed to `cron`, `at` or a user service is in no process tree of the runner's at all. Detection at the signature is the layer that works on every harness. This is from knowledge, not run.
  - **Residual, for the fragment.** *A control changed while the run is parked is named on the next entry and in the feed, and the run does not park on it, whether a person or a process an earlier iteration left behind changed it. A control changed between two runner processes — after a `--no-park` exit, a spent budget, a stop — is not seen by the runner at all; `git status` shows it, except under a `tools/` directory Git ignores.* The fragment's controls bullet also gains the positive sentence: a control changed between two iterations parks the run.
  - **Deferred to the Parking Lot, for the completion audit:** containing what an iteration leaves behind (the Linux-only routes above); a comparison across runner processes; the runner saying that a process outside the iteration's group was left (today's *left a process running* line did not fire in the reproduction).
  - **Level.** No raise. The slice is already a MINOR at `1.6.0.dev0`, and an optional entry field is within it. No new fragment: `changelog.d/runner-bookkeeping.md` gains the sentences above, and its catch-up paragraph gains *an entry without `controls_changed_between` is read as it always was*.
  - **Where it is written in the source.** `assets/toolkit/scripts/agents/cruise.py` (`drive()`, the comment over `CONTROL_PATHS`), and the generated cruise command's *Blocked* paragraph at its source, which reaches `delivery/` through `slipwai migrate` (owner priority 3; nothing in `delivery/.written` is edited by hand).
  - **Criteria:** numbered by the host as AC-S02-70 to AC-S02-79, under S02-runner-bookkeeping in the specification.
- **Why:** The developer's reason to leave a run alone is that the run cannot quietly change what judges it. Today one detached process does exactly that.
  - **What the reproduction showed.** The runner goes green for three iterations and the feed and the log say nothing. The only trace is `git status`, and an edit under an ignored `tools/` directory would not show there either.
